y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{L \cup\{(x,y)\}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}.

(B.47)

Similarly, define
W^{R}
be the linear map such that for any
(L,R)\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\dist}
and
y\in[N]
such that
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)
,

\displaystyle W^{R}\cdot\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{L}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\absolutevalue{L \cup R}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[N]:\\
x\not\in\Dom(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{L}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket*{R \cup\{(x,y)\}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}.

(B.48)

Definition 29
.

The partial path-recording oracle is the operator
W
defined as

W\coloneqq W^{L}+W^{R,\dagger}.

(B.49)

We now extend the definition of
W
in
[
42
]
to define its conjugate
\overline{W}
. Note that
\overline{W}
is not the exact complex conjugate of
W
. However, intuitively speaking, for an oracle adversary,
\overline{W}
will behave like the complex conjugation of
W
similar to how
\overline{V}
behave like the complex conjugation of
V
.

Definition 30

(Conjugates of
W^{L},W^{R},
and
W
)
.

Define
\overline{W}^{L}
to be the linear map such that for any
(L,R)\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\dist}
and
x\in[N]
such that
x\not\in\Dom(L\cup R)
,

\displaystyle\overline{W}^{L}\cdot\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{L}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\absolutevalue{L \cup R}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in[N]:\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket*{R \cup\{(x,y)\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}.

(B.50)

Similarly, define
\overline{W}^{R}
be the linear map such that for any
(L,R)\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\dist}
and
y\in[N]
such that
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)
,

\displaystyle\overline{W}^{R}\cdot\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{L}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\absolutevalue{L \cup R}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[N]:\\
x\not\in\Dom(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{L\cup\{(x,y)\}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}.

(B.51)

The conjugate of the partial path-recording oracle
W
is the operator
\overline{W}
defined as

\overline{W}\coloneqq\overline{W}^{L}+\overline{W}^{R,\dagger}.

(B.52)

We instantiate the following definitions of projectors.

Definition 31

(Bijective-relation projectors)
.

Define the projectors

\displaystyle\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\coloneq\sum_{(L,R)\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\dist}}\outerproduct*{L}{L}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\otimes\outerproduct*{R}{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}},\quad\quad\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\coloneq\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\cdot\Pi_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}=\Pi_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}},

(B.53)

where the projector
\Pi_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}
is the maximum-length projector defined in
Definition

17
.

Definition 32
.

For a partial isometry
G
, let
\mathcal{D}(G)
and
\mathcal{I}(G)
denote its domain and image. Let
\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(G)}=G^{\dagger}\cdot G
and
\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(G)}=G\cdot G^{\dagger}
denote the orthogonal projectors onto
\mathcal{D}(G)
and
\mathcal{I}(G)
.

Because the conjugated versions of
W^{L},W^{R},
and
W
amounts to swapping the
L
and
R
register, the proofs in
[
42
]
can be combined with the action of
\mathsf{pfO}^{*}
and
\mathsf{pfO}^{T}
to establish the following lemmas.

Lemma 17

(
W
is a restriction of
\mathsf{pfO}
up to isometry; From Claim 13 of
[
42
]
)
.

We have

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{pfO}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W)},

(B.54)

\displaystyle W^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{pfO}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W)}

(B.55)

\displaystyle\overline{W}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{pfO}^{*}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W})},

(B.56)

\displaystyle\overline{W}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{pfO}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W})}.

(B.57)

Lemma 18

(From Fact 5 of
[
42
]
)
.

For any integer
i\geq 0
,
W^{L},W^{R},\overline{W}^{L},\overline{W}^{R}
map states in the subspace associated to the projector
\mathbbm{1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{\leq i,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}
into the subspace associated with the projector
\mathbbm{1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{\leq i+1,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}
.

Lemma 19

(From Claim 9 and Claim 11 of
[
42
]
)
.

W^{L},W^{R},W,\overline{W}^{L},\overline{W}^{R},\overline{W}
are partial isometries.

Lemma 20

(From Fact 8 of
[
42
]
)
.

The domain and image of the partial isometry
W
are given by

\displaystyle\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W)}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W^{L})}+\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W^{R})},

(B.58)

\displaystyle\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W)}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W^{R})}+\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W^{L})}.

(B.59)

The domain and image of the partial isometry
\overline{W}
are given by

\displaystyle\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W})}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}^{L})}+\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W}^{R})},

(B.60)

\displaystyle\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W})}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}^{R})}+\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W}^{L})}.

(B.61)

Lemma 21

(From Claim 10 and Claim 12 of
[
42
]
)
.

For all integers
t\geq 0
,

\Pi_{\leq t}
commutes with
\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W^{L})}
,
\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W^{L})}
,
\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W^{R})}
,
\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W^{R})}
,
\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W)}
,
\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W)}
,
\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}^{L})}
,
\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W}^{L})}
,
\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}^{R})}
,
\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W}^{R})}
,
\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W})}
, and
\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W})}
.

Lemma 22

(
W
is a restriction of
V
; From Claim 17 of
[
42
]
)
.

We have

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=V\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W)},

(B.62)

\displaystyle W^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=V^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W)},

(B.63)

\displaystyle\overline{W}

\displaystyle=\overline{V}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W})},

(B.64)

\displaystyle\overline{W}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\overline{V}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W})}.

(B.65)

(From Corollary 8.3 of
[
42
]
)
.

\displaystyle W^{\dagger}\cdot V

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W)},

(B.66)

\displaystyle W\cdot V^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W)},

(B.67)

\displaystyle\overline{W}^{\dagger}\cdot\overline{V}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W})},

(B.68)

\displaystyle\overline{W}\cdot\overline{V}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W})}.

(B.69)

Lemma 24

(Twirling by strong approximate unitary
2
-design; From Lemma 9.2 of
[
42
]
)
.

For any strong approximate unitary
2
-design
\mathfrak{D}
with additive error
\varepsilon
, and any integer
0\leq t\leq N-1
, we have

\displaystyle\norm{ \E_{C,D \sim\mathfrak{D}} (C_{\gsA} \otimes Q[C,D]_{\gsL\gsR})^\dagger\cdot\Big( \Pi^{\bij}_{\leq t, \gsL\gsR} - \Pi^{\calD(W)}_{\leq t, \gsA\gsL\gsR}\Big) \cdot(C_{\gsA} \otimes Q[C,D]_{\gsL\gsR}) }_{C,D\sim\mathfrak{D}}(C_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes Q[C,D]_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}})^{\dagger}\cdot\Big(\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}-\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W)}_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\Big)\cdot(C_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes Q[C,D]_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}})_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 6t\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+2t\varepsilon,

(B.70)

\displaystyle\norm{ \E_{C,D \sim\mathfrak{D}} (D^\dagger_{\gsA} \otimes Q[C,D]_{\gsL\gsR})^\dagger\cdot\Big( \Pi^{\bij}_{\leq t, \gsL\gsR} - \Pi^{\calI(W)}_{\leq t, \gsA\gsL\gsR}\Big) \cdot(D^\dagger_{\gsA} \otimes Q[C,D]_{\gsL\gsR}) }_{C,D\sim\mathfrak{D}}(D^{\dagger}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes Q[C,D]_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}})^{\dagger}\cdot\Big(\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}-\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W)}_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\Big)\cdot(D^{\dagger}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes Q[C,D]_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}})_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 6t\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+2t\varepsilon,

(B.71)

\displaystyle\norm{ \E_{C,D \sim\mathfrak{D}} (C^*_{\gsA} \otimes Q[C,D]_{\gsL\gsR})^\dagger\cdot\Big( \Pi^{\bij}_{\leq t, \gsL\gsR} - \Pi^{\calD(\overline{W})}_{\leq t, \gsA\gsL\gsR}\Big) \cdot(C^*_{\gsA} \otimes Q[C,D]_{\gsL\gsR}) }_{C,D\sim\mathfrak{D}}(C^{*}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes Q[C,D]_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}})^{\dagger}\cdot\Big(\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}-\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W})}_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\Big)\cdot(C^{*}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes Q[C,D]_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}})_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 6t\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+2t\varepsilon,

(B.72)

\displaystyle\norm{ \E_{C,D \sim\mathfrak{D}} (D^T_{\gsA} \otimes Q[C,D]_{\gsL\gsR})^\dagger\cdot\Big( \Pi^{\bij}_{\leq t, \gsL\gsR} - \Pi^{\calI(\overline{W})}_{\leq t, \gsA\gsL\gsR}\Big) \cdot(D^T_{\gsA} \otimes Q[C,D]_{\gsL\gsR}) }_{C,D\sim\mathfrak{D}}(D^{T}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes Q[C,D]_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}})^{\dagger}\cdot\Big(\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}-\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W})}_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\Big)\cdot(D^{T}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes Q[C,D]_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}})_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 6t\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+2t\varepsilon.

(B.73)

Proof.

The proof follows from the proof of Lemma 9.2 in
[
99
]
with one replacement.
In Claims 29 and 30 and Eq. (11.82),
[
99
]
uses the following spectral norm bound for the twirl over an
exact
unitary 2-design
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\E_{U\sim\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}\left[(U\otimes U)^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}\cdot(U\otimes U)\right]\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{N+1},

(B.74)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\E_{U\sim\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}\left[(U\otimes U^{*})\cdot(\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}-\Pi^{\mathsf{EPR}})\cdot(U\otimes U^{*})\right]\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N+1}.

(B.75)

Here, we replace these bounds with analogous bounds for the twirl over an
approximate
unitary 2-design
\mathfrak{D}
.
For any (standard) approximate unitary 2-design with additive error
\varepsilon
, the first inequality becomes,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\E_{U\sim\mathfrak{D}}\left[(U\otimes U)^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}\cdot(U\otimes U)\right]\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{N+1}+\varepsilon.

(B.76)

Meanwhile, for any strong approximate unitary 2-design with additive error
\varepsilon
, the second inequality becomes,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\E_{U\sim\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}\left[(U\otimes U^{*})\cdot(\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}-\Pi^{\mathsf{EPR}})\cdot(U\otimes U^{*})\right]\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N+1}+\varepsilon.

(B.77)

To derive the first inequality, we abbreviate
\Phi_{\mathfrak{D}}(X)\equiv\E_{U\sim\mathfrak{D}}\left[(U\otimes U)X(U\otimes U)^{\dagger}\right]
and
\delta\Phi_{\mathfrak{D}}\equiv\Phi_{\mathfrak{D}}-\Phi_{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}
, and apply

\lVert\delta\Phi^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{D}}(O)\rVert_{\infty}=\max_{\rho}\text{tr}(\delta\Phi^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{D}}(O)\rho)=\max_{\rho}\text{tr}(O\delta\Phi_{\mathfrak{D}}(\rho))\leq\max_{\rho}\lVert O\rVert_{\infty}\cdot\lVert\Phi_{\mathfrak{D}}(\rho)\rVert_{1}\leq\lVert O\rVert_{\infty}\cdot\varepsilon
.
An identical series of steps derives the second inequality.
Propagating this replacement through the remainder of the proof yields Lemma
24
.
∎

Note that in the statement of
Lemma

24
,
\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}
is shorthand for
\mathbbm{1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{\leq t,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}
, and thus the operators inside the spectral norm
\norm{\cdot}_{\infty}
act on
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}
.

B.6
V,\overline{V}
approximates Haar-random unitary
U
under
U,U^{\dagger},U^{*},U^{T}

Because all lemmas generalize to complex conjugation and transpose using the suitably defined
\overline{W}
and
\overline{V}
, we can follow the same proof of
[
42
]
to show that the extended path recording oracle
V,\overline{V}
approximates the following random unitary ensemble under
U,U^{\dagger},U^{*},U^{T}
.

Definition 33

(
\mathsf{sPFC}(\mathfrak{D})
distribution)
.

For any distribution
\mathfrak{D}
supported on
\mathcal{U}(N)
, define the distribution
\mathsf{sPFC}({\mathfrak{D}})
as follows:

1.

Sample a uniformly random permutation
\pi\sim\mathsf{Sym}_{N}
, a uniformly random
f\sim\{0,1,2\}^{N}
, and two independently sampled
n
-qubit unitaries
C,D\sim\mathfrak{D}
. Following the definitions in
Section

B.3
,

\displaystyle F_{f}\coloneqq\sum_{x\in[N]}e^{2\pi\cdot f(x)\cdot i/3}\outerproduct*{x}{x}\quad\text{and}\quad P_{\pi}\coloneqq\sum_{x\in[N]}\outerproduct*{\pi(x)}{x}.

(B.78)

2.

Output the
n
-qubit unitary
\mathcal{O}\coloneqq D\cdot P_{\pi}\cdot F_{f}\cdot C
.

Definition 34

(Global state after queries to
V,\overline{V}
)
.

For a
t
-query oracle adversary
\mathcal{A}
that can perform queries to
U,U^{\dagger},U^{*},U^{T}
, where
b_{i}\in\{0,1\}
and
c_{i}\in\{0,1\}
denote the four choices (
U\rightarrow b_{i}=0,c_{i}=0;U^{\dagger}\rightarrow b_{i}=1,c_{i}=0;U^{*}\rightarrow b_{i}=0,c_{i}=1;U^{T}\rightarrow b_{i}=1,c_{i}=1
), and any
0\leq i\leq t
, let

\displaystyle\ket*{\calA_i^{V, \overline{V}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\coloneqq\prod_{i=1}^{t}

\displaystyle\Bigg(\Big((1-c_{i})((1-b_{i})\cdot V_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}+b_{i}\cdot V_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}^{\dagger})

(B.79)

\displaystyle+c_{i}((1-b_{i})\cdot\overline{V}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}+b_{i}\cdot\overline{V}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}^{\dagger})\Big)\cdot A_{i,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}}\Bigg)\ket*{0^{n+m}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}}\otimes\ket*{\varnothing}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket*{\varnothing}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}

(B.80)

denote the global state on registers
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}
after
\mathcal{A}
makes
i
queries to
V
.

We will also consider the global purified state after queries to
W,\overline{W}
, where we twirl the input and the output states by two independent random unitaries sampled from any unitary
2
-design. For this purpose, we define the purification of two random unitaries
C,D
.

Definition 35
.

For any distribution
\mathfrak{D}
over
n
-qubit unitaries, define the state

\displaystyle\ket*{\init(\mathfrak{D})}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\coloneq\int_{C,D}\sqrt{d\mu_{\mathfrak{D}}(C)d\mu_{\mathfrak{D}}(D)}\ket*{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}}\otimes\ket*{D}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}},

(B.81)

where
\mu_{\mathfrak{D}}(C)
is the probability measure for which
C
is sampled from
\mathfrak{D}
.

Definition 36

(Controlled
C,D
and
Q
)
.

Define the following operators

\displaystyle\mathsf{cC}\coloneq\int_{C}C_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes\outerproduct*{C}{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}},\quad\mathsf{cD}\coloneq\int_{D}D_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\otimes\outerproduct*{D}{D}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}},

(B.82)

\displaystyle\mathsf{cQ}\coloneq\int_{C,D}Q[C,D]_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5},}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\otimes\outerproduct*{C}{C}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}}\otimes\outerproduct*{D}{D}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}.

(B.83)

Definition 37

(Global state after queries to Twirled
W,\overline{W}
)
.

For a
t
-query adversary
\mathcal{A}
that can perform queries to
U,U^{\dagger},U^{*},U^{T}
, where
b_{i}\in\{0,1\}
and
c_{i}\in\{0,1\}
denote the four choices (
U\rightarrow b_{i}=0,c_{i}=0;U^{\dagger}\rightarrow b_{i}=1,c_{i}=0;U^{*}\rightarrow b_{i}=0,c_{i}=1;U^{T}\rightarrow b_{i}=1,c_{i}=1
), let

\displaystyle\ket*{\mathcal{A}_0^{W, \overline{W},\frakD}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\ket*{0^n}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{0^m}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}}\ket*{\varnothing}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket*{\varnothing}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{init}(\mathfrak{D})}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}.

(B.84)

For
i
from
1
to
t
, let

\displaystyle\ket*{\mathcal{A}_i^{W, \overline{W},\frakD}}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\Big((1-c_{i})((1-b_{i})\cdot(\mathsf{cD}\cdot W\cdot\mathsf{cC})+b_{i}\cdot(\mathsf{cD}\cdot W\cdot\mathsf{cC})^{\dagger})+

(B.85)

\displaystyle\quad c_{i}((1-b_{i})\cdot(\mathsf{cD}^{*}\cdot\overline{W}\cdot\mathsf{cC}^{*})+b_{i}\cdot(\mathsf{cD}^{*}\cdot\overline{W}\cdot\mathsf{cC}^{*})^{\dagger})\Big)\cdot A_{i}\cdot\ket*{\mathcal{A}^{W,\frakD}_{i-1}}.

(B.86)

Lemma 25

(
W
is indistinguishable from
V
after twirling; From Lemma 9.3 of
[
42
]
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{D}
be any strong approximate unitary
2
-design with additive error
\varepsilon
. For any
t
-query oracle adversary
\mathcal{A}
that can query
\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\dagger},\mathcal{O}^{*},\mathcal{O}^{T}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}(\Tr_{-\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\outerproduct*{\mathcal{A}^{W, \overline{W}, \mathfrak{D}}_t}{\mathcal{A}^{W, \overline{W}, \mathfrak{D}}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}},\Tr_{-\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\outerproduct*{\mathcal{A}^{V, \overline{V}}_t}{\mathcal{A}^{V, \overline{V}}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}})\leq\frac{9t}{N^{1/8}}+2t^{1/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}.

(B.87)

Proof.

The proof follows from the proof of Lemma 9.3 in
[
99
]
with one replacement. In the application of Lemma 9.2 in Eq. (9.45) of the proof of Claim 18, we apply Lemma
24
for the twirl over a strong
\varepsilon
-approximate unitary 2-design instead. This modifies the right hand side of the statement of Claim 18 to
1-35t^{2}/N^{1/4}-\sqrt{2t\varepsilon}
. Propagating this replacement through the rest of the proof of Lemma 9.3 and applying the inequality
\sqrt{x+y}\leq\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y}
yields Lemma
25
.
∎

Lemma 26

(
\mathsf{sPFC}(\mathfrak{D})
is indistinguishable from
V
; From Lemma 9.1 of
[
42
]
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{D}
be any strong approximate unitary
2
-design with additive error
\varepsilon
. For any
t
-query oracle adversary
\mathcal{A}
that can query
\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\dagger},\mathcal{O}^{*},\mathcal{O}^{T}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}\left(\E_{\mathcal{O}\sim\mathsf{sPFC}(\mathfrak{D})}\outerproduct*{\mathcal{A}_t^{\calO}}{\mathcal{A}_t^{\calO}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}},\,\,\,\Tr_{\mathsf{L}\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\mathcal{A}^{V, \overline{V}}_t}{\mathcal{A}^{V, \overline{V}}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{9t(t+1)}{N^{1/8}}+4t^{5/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}.

(B.88)

Proof.

The proof follows from the proof of Lemma 9.1 in
[
99
]
. The right hand side of the statement of Lemma 9.4 is modified to
1-70t^{2}/N^{1/4}-2\sqrt{2t\varepsilon}
following the modification of Claim 18. From this, the right hand side of the statement of Lemma 9.5 is modified to
9t^{2}/N^{1/8}+t\sqrt{2\sqrt{2t\varepsilon}}\leq 9t^{2}/N^{1/8}+2t^{5/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}
. Inserting this modification and that of Lemma 25 into the proof of Lemma 9.1 in
[
99
]
yields Lemma
26
.
∎

Theorem 10

(
V
is indistinguishable from a Haar-random unitary; From Theorem 8 of
[
42
]
)
.

For any
t
-query oracle adversary
\mathcal{A}
that can can query
\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\dagger},\mathcal{O}^{*},\mathcal{O}^{T}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}\left(\E_{\mathcal{O}\sim\mu_{\mathsf{Haar}}}\outerproduct*{\mathcal{A}_t^{\calO}}{\mathcal{A}_t^{\calO}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}},\,\,\,\Tr_{\mathsf{L}\mathsf{R}}\left(\outerproduct*{\mathcal{A}^{V, \overline{V}}_t}{\mathcal{A}^{V, \overline{V}}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{9t(t+1)}{N^{1/8}}.

(B.89)

To simplify the notation, we will often denote
\ket*{\mathcal{A}^{V, \overline{V}}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}
as simply
\ket*{\mathcal{A}^{V}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}
. Similarly, we will denote
\ket*{\mathcal{A}^{W, \overline{W}, \mathfrak{D}}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}
as simply
\ket*{\mathcal{A}^{W, \mathfrak{D}}_t}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}
when appropriate.

Appendix C
The Luby-Rackoff-Function-Clifford (LRFC) ensemble

In this section we present the central random unitary construction of this work, the Luby-Rackoff-Function-Clifford (LRFC) ensemble, which only uses random unitary
2
-designs and random functions. The LRFC ensemble does not require the use of random permutations, which is useful for generating minimum depth strong random unitaries, due to the lack of known low-depth constructions of quantum-secure pseudorandom permutations. The best known construction of quantum-secure strong pseudorandom permutations is given in
[
73
]
, which requires
\poly(n)
circuit depth for
n
-qubit systems.

In what follows, we first define the LRFC ensemble and then proceed step-by-step through our proof that it is indistinguishable from a Haar-random unitary.

C.1
Definition of the ensemble

We begin by formally defining the LRFC ensemble and its key ingredients.

Feistel network.

Let
n
be the number of qubits and
N\coloneqq 2^{n}
. Our construction utilizes a simple variant of the Feistel network, also known as the Luby-Rackoff construction
[
75
]
. For a function
h:\{0,1\}^{n/2}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{n/2}
, we define the
Left
and
Right
Luby-Rackoff function as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}_{h}(x_{<}\|x_{>})

\displaystyle\coloneqq(x_{<}\oplus h(x_{>}))\|x_{>},

(C.1)

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{h}(x_{<}\|x_{>})

\displaystyle\coloneqq x_{<}\|(x_{>}\oplus h(x_{<})),

(C.2)

where
x=x_{<}\|x_{>}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, and
\|
denotes bitstring concatenation.

Quantum oracles.

We define the following
n
-qubit quantum oracles:

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{f}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}e^{2\pi if(x)/3}\outerproduct{x}{x},

(C.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\mathsf{L},h_{1}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\outerproduct{\mathsf{L}_{h_1}(x)}{x}=\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\outerproduct{(x_< \oplus h_1(x_>)) \| x_>}{x},

(C.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\mathsf{R},h_{2}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\outerproduct{\mathsf{R}_{h_2}(x)}{x}=\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\outerproduct{x_< \| (x_> \oplus h_2(x_<))}{x}.

(C.5)

These are identical to the operators
F
,
S_{L}
,
S_{R}
defined in the main text.

Construction.

Let
C,D
be two
n
-qubit random unitaries sampled independently from a unitary 2-design, such as a random Clifford circuit. A random unitary
U
sampled from the LRFC ensemble is given by:

U\coloneqq D\cdot\mathcal{O}^{\mathsf{R},h_{2}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{\mathsf{L},h_{1}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{f}\cdot C.

(C.6)

Putting everything together, we have the following definition for LRFC ensemble.

Definition 38

(LRFC ensemble)
.

Suppose
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
are drawn uniformly randomly from functions on
\{0,1\}^{n/2}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{n/2}
,
f
is drawn uniformly randomly from ternary functions on
\{0,1\}^{n}
, and
C,D
are drawn uniformly from a unitary
2
-design on
n
qubits.
Then the Luby-Rackoff-Function-Clifford (LRFC) ensemble is given by the family of
n
-qubit unitaries:

U:=\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}e^{2\pi if(x)/3}\cdot D\cdot\outerproduct{x_< \oplus h_1(x_>) \| x_> \oplus h_2(x_< \oplus h_1(x_>))}{x}\cdot C,

(C.7)

where
\lVert
denotes the concatenation of two
\frac{n}{2}
-bit strings and
x=x_{<}\lVert x_{>}
.

C.2
Purified Luby-Rackoff-Function oracle

In this section, we analyze the view of an adversary that makes queries to an oracle implementing the Luby-Rackoff-Function construction with random functions
h_{1}
,
h_{2}
, and a random ternary function
f
. We will do this by analyzing the
purified Luby-Rackoff-Function oracle
, which uses a purification of these random functions.

Definition 39

(Purified Luby-Rackoff-Function oracle)
.

The purified Luby-Rackoff-Function oracle
\mathsf{lrfO}
is a unitary acting on registers
\mathsf{A}
,
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{1}}
,
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{2}}
,
\mathsf{F}
, where

•

\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{1}}
is a register associated with the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{1}}}
, defined to be the span of the orthonormal states
\ket{h_1}
for all
h_{1}:\{0,1\}^{n/2}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{n/2}
.

•

\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{2}}
is a register associated with the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{2}}}
, defined to be the span of the orthonormal states
\ket{h_2}
for all
h_{2}:\{0,1\}^{n/2}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{n/2}
.

•

\mathsf{F}
is a register associated with the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{F}}
, defined to be the span of the orthonormal states
\ket{f}
for all
f:\{0,1\}^{n}\rightarrow\{0,1,2\}
.

The unitary
\mathsf{lrfO}
is defined to act as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}\ket{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{h_1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}}}\ket{h_2}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}}}\ket{f}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}

(C.8)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\omega_{3}^{f(x)}\ket{x_< \oplus h_1(x_>) \| x_> \oplus h_2(x_< \oplus h_1(x_>))}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{h_1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}\ket{h_2}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}}\ket{f}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}},

(C.9)

for all
x=x_{<}\|x_{>}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
with
x_{<},x_{>}\in\{0,1\}^{n/2}
, and for all functions
h_{1},h_{2}:\{0,1\}^{n/2}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{n/2}
,
f:\{0,1\}^{n}\rightarrow\{0,1,2\}
. Here,
\omega_{3}=\exp(2 \pi i / 3)
.

The action of
\mathsf{lrfO}^{*}
is

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}^{*}\ket{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{h_1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}}}\ket{h_2}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}}}\ket{f}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}

(C.10)

\displaystyle=\omega_{3}^{-f(x)}\ket{x_< \oplus h_1(x_>) \| (x_> \oplus h_2(x_< \oplus h_1(x_>)))}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{h_1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}\ket{h_2}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}}\ket{f}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}.

(C.11)

The action of
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger}
is

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger}\ket{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{h_1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}}}\ket{h_2}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}}}\ket{f}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}

(C.12)

\displaystyle=\omega_{3}^{-f(x)}\ket{ (y_< \oplus h_1(y_> \oplus h_2(y_<))) \| y_> \oplus h_2(y_<) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{h_1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}\ket{h_2}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}}\ket{f}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}.

(C.13)

The action of
\mathsf{lrfO}^{T}
is

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}^{T}\ket{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{h_1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}}}\ket{h_2}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}}}\ket{f}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}

(C.14)

\displaystyle=\omega_{3}^{f(x)}\ket{ (y_< \oplus h_1(y_> \oplus h_2(y_<))) \| y_> \oplus h_2(y_<) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{h_1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}\ket{h_2}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}}\ket{f}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}.

(C.15)

Because
\mathsf{lrfO}
is constructed by purifying the randomness inf
h_{1},h_{2},f
, the output state of any oracle adversary that queries the purified oracle after tracing out
\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{H}_{2},\mathsf{F}
is equivalent to the output state of the adversary that queries the standard oracle
\mathcal{O}^{\mathsf{R},h_{2}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{\mathsf{L},h_{1}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{f}
, for uniformly random
h_{1},h_{2}\sim\{0,1\}^{n/2\cdot\sqrt{N}}
and
f\sim\{0,1,2\}^{N}
.

Fact 2

(Equivalence of purified and standard oracles)
.

For any oracle adversary, the following oracle instantiations are perfectly indistinguishable:

•

(Queries to a random
\mathcal{O}^{\mathsf{R},h_{2}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{\mathsf{L},h_{1}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{f}
) Sample a uniformly random
h_{1},h_{2}\sim\{0,1\}^{n/2\cdot\sqrt{N}},f\sim\{0,1,2\}^{N}
. On each query, apply
U=\mathcal{O}^{\mathsf{R},h_{2}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{\mathsf{L},h_{1}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{f},U^{\dagger},U^{*}
or
U^{T}
to register
\mathsf{A}
.

•

(Queries to
\mathsf{lrfO}
) Initialize registers
\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{H}_{2},\mathsf{F}
to
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}^{\sqrt{N}}}\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}:\{0,1\}^{n/2}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{n/2}}\ket{h_1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}\otimes\ket{h_2}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}\otimes\frac{1}{\sqrt{3^{N}}}\sum_{f\in\{0,1,2\}^{N}}\ket*{f}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}
. On each query, apply
\mathsf{lrfO},\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger},\mathsf{lrfO}^{*}
or
\mathsf{lrfO}^{T}
to registers
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{H}_{2},\mathsf{F}
.

Next, we define the relation states for the LRF oracle.

Definition 40

(
\mathsf{lrf}
-relation state)
.

For the relations

L=\{(x_{1},y_{1}),\dots,(x_{\ell},y_{\ell})\}\in\mathcal{L}_{\ell}
and
R=\{(x^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime}_{1}),\dots,(x^{\prime}_{r},y^{\prime}_{r})\}\in\mathcal{R}_{r}
,
where
\ell
and
r
are non-negative integers such that
\ell+r\leq 2^{n/2}
, let

\displaystyle\ket{\mathrm{lrf}_{L,R}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\sqrt{N}^{\sqrt{N}-\ell-r}}}\sum_{h_{1}:\{0,1\}^{n/2}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{n/2}}\delta_{h_{1},L\cup R}\ket{h_1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}

(C.16)

\displaystyle\otimes\frac{1}{\sqrt{\sqrt{N}^{\sqrt{N}-\ell-r}}}\sum_{h_{2}:\{0,1\}^{n/2}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{n/2}}\delta^{\prime}_{h_{2},L\cup R}\ket{h_2}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}}

(C.17)

\displaystyle\otimes\frac{1}{\sqrt{3^{N}}}\sum_{f:\{0,1\}^{n}\rightarrow\{0,1,2\}}\omega_{3}^{\sum_{(x,y)\in L}f(x)-\sum_{(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in R}f(x^{\prime})}\ket{f}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}.

(C.18)

Here,
\delta_{h_{1},L\cup R}
is an indicator variable that equals
1
if
y_{<}=(x_{<}\oplus h_{1}(x_{>}))
for all
(x,y)\in L\cup R
, and
0
otherwise;
\delta^{\prime}_{h_{2},L\cup R}
equals
1
if
(y_{>}\oplus h_{2}(y_{<}))=x_{>}
for all
(x,y)\in L\cup R
, and
0
otherwise.

C.3
Projecting onto the local distinct subspace

Our analysis of the LRFC ensemble centers upon a projection onto the
local distinct subspace
on the registers
\mathsf{L}
and
\mathsf{R}
.
In this section, we first define this subspace and then show that we can project onto this subspace (and close variants of it) throughout any quantum experiment that queries a Haar-random unitary or the LRFC ensemble.
These latter steps form the core technical results behind our proof that the LRFC ensemble is indistinguishable from a Haar-random unitary.

We define the local distinct subspace as follows.

Definition 41
.

Let
\mathcal{R}^{2,\lcdist}
be the set of all ordered pairs of relations
(L,R)\in\mathcal{R}^{2}
where
L\cup R=\{(x_{1},y_{1}),\dots,(x_{t},y_{t})\}
satisfies
x_{1,>},\ldots,x_{t,>}
are all distinct and
y_{1,<},\ldots,y_{t,<}
are all distinct.

The states
\ket{\mathrm{lrf}_{L,R}}
possess several nice properties when
L
and
R
are locally distinct.
For example, by expanding the definition of
\ket{\mathrm{lrf}_{L,R}}
, we obtain the following facts.

Fact 3

(Orthonormality)
.

\{\ket{\mathsf{lrf}_{L,R}}\}_{(L,R)\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\lcdist}}
forms an orthonormal set of vectors.

Fact 4

(Action of
\mathsf{lrfO}
)
.

For any
(L,R)\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\lcdist}
and
x\in[N]
such that
x_{>}\not\in\Dom_{>}(L\cup R)
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L,R}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{y\in[N]}\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L \cup\{(x, y)\}, R}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}},

(C.19)

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}^{*}\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L,R}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{y\in[N]}\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L, R \cup\{(x, y)\}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}.

(C.20)

Similarly, for any
(L,R)\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\lcdist}
and
y\in[N]
such that
y_{<}\not\in\imaginary_{<}(L\cup R)
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger}\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L,R}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{x\in[N]}\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L, R \cup\{(x, y)\}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}},

(C.21)

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}^{T}\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L,R}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{x\in[N]}\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L \cup\{(x, y)\}, R}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}.

(C.22)

We can also define a partial isometry between the states
\ket{\mathrm{lrf}_{L,R}}
, and the states
\ket{L}\otimes\ket{R}
, when
L
and
R
are locally distinct.

Definition 42
.

Define the partial isometry
\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LRF}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{1}}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{2}}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{F}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{L}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{R}}
to be

\displaystyle\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LRF}}\coloneqq\sum_{(L,R)\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\lcdist}}\ket*{L}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\otimes\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\cdot\bra{\mathsf{lrf}_{L, R}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}.

(C.23)

Note that
\mathsf{Compress}
is a partial isometry by
3
.

For our later analysis, it will be convenient to define projectors that keep one in the locally distinct subspace.
We do so as follows.
First, we recall the definition of the projector onto
bijective
relation states,

\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}=\begin{cases}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}},&\text{if }\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)\in\mathsf{distinct},\\
&\text{and }\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)\in\mathsf{distinct}\\
0,&\text{else}.\\
\end{cases}

(C.24)

In a similar fashion, we define the not-in-domain projector on
\mathsf{ALR}
as,

\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\ket{ x }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}=\begin{cases}\ket{ x }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}},&\text{if }x\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R),\\
0,&\text{else},\\
\end{cases}

(C.25)

and the not-in-image projector as,

\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Im}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\ket{ y }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}=\begin{cases}\ket{ y }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}},&\text{if }y\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R),\\
0,&\text{else}.\\
\end{cases}

(C.26)

Turning to the local distinct projectors, we define the
locally bijective
relation states via the projector,

\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}=\begin{cases}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}},&\text{if }\mathsf{Dom}_{>}(L\cup R)\in\mathsf{distinct},\\
&\text{and }\mathsf{Im}_{<}(L\cup R)\in\mathsf{distinct}\\
0,&\text{else},\\
\end{cases}

(C.27)

and the not-in-local-domain and not-in-local-image projectors as,

\displaystyle\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\ket{ x }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\ket{ x }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}},&\text{if }x_{>}\notin\mathsf{Dom}_{>}(L\cup R),\\
0,&\text{else},\\
\end{cases}

(C.28)

\displaystyle\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\ket{ y }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\ket{ y }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}},&\text{if }y_{<}\notin\mathsf{Im}_{<}(L\cup R),\\
0,&\text{else}.\\
\end{cases}

(C.29)

We also let,

\displaystyle\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}

\displaystyle=1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}+\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}

(C.30)

\displaystyle\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Im}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locIm}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}

\displaystyle=1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Im}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}+\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}},

(C.31)

denote projectors which do nothing if
x\in\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)
, and project to the not-in-local-domain subspace if
x\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)
(and similar for
\mathsf{Im}
).

C.3.1
Twirled
W
is indistinguishable from twirled projected
W

In this and the following two sections, we use the local distinct subspace projectors can be inserted, up to small error, throughout any quantum experiment that queries the LRFC ensemble or a Haar-random unitary.
We begin in this section by analyzing the insertion of the local distinct subspace projectors for experiments that involve the path-recording oracles
W
and
\overline{W}
.

We define projected versions of the path-recording oracles
W
and
\overline{W}
as follows,

\displaystyle W^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot W\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}

(C.32)

\displaystyle(W^{\dagger})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot W^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Im}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locIm}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}

(C.33)

\displaystyle\overline{W}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\overline{W}\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}

(C.34)

\displaystyle(\overline{W}^{\dagger})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\overline{W}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Im}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locIm}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}.

(C.35)

In the remainder of this section, we show that the projected oracles are indistinguishable from the original oracles whenever the oracles are surrounded by random unitary 2-designs.

Lemma 27

(Twirled
W
is indistinguishable from twirled
W^{\prime}
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{D}
be any strong approximate unitary 2-design with additive error
\varepsilon
. For any
t
-query oracle adversary
\mathcal{A}
, we have

\mathsf{TD}\left(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W, \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}},\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W', \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\right)\leq\frac{\sqrt{70}t(t-1)}{N^{1/8}}+\frac{2t(t+1)}{N^{1/4}}+4t^{5/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}.

(C.36)

Here and in the remainder of the manuscript, we abbreviate
\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W,\overline{W}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }
as simply
\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W, \mathfrak{D}}_t }
.
All of our analysis includes queries to the conjugate and transpose.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{TD}_{t}
denote the trace distance in Eq. (
C.36
). We will prove the theorem by induction.
The statement holds trivially at
t=0
.
To prove the inductive step, suppose that the Eq. (
C.36
) holds up to time
t-1
for any
t\geq 1
.
Without loss of generality, we assume that the oracle
W
is applied at time
t
.
The case when
W^{\dagger}
,
\overline{W}
, and
\overline{W}^{\dagger}
are applied follow by symmetric arguments.
The states at time
t
are obtained from the states at time
t-1
as follows,

\displaystyle\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W, \mathfrak{D}}_{t} }

\displaystyle=\mathsf{cD}\cdot W\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W, \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }

(C.37)

\displaystyle\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W', \mathfrak{D}}_{t} }

\displaystyle=\mathsf{cD}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot W\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }

(C.38)

In the second line, we use that
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\ket*{\mathcal{A}^{W',\mathfrak{D}}_{t-1}}=\ket*{\mathcal{A}^{W',\mathfrak{D}}_{t-1}}
to eliminate the final projector in
W^{\prime}
.
This follows because the projector
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}
has already been applied after the prior
(t-1)
-th query.
We have

\begin{split}\mathsf{TD}_{t}\leq\mathsf{TD}_{t-1}&+2\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W, \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\\
&\quad+2\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\big)\cdot W\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2},\\
\end{split}

(C.39)

where the first term accounts for the error up to time
t-1
, the second term for the error induced by the projector
\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}
[using Eq. (
B.18
)], and the third term for the error induced by the projector
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}
[again using Eq. (
B.18
)].

We bound the second term as follows.
From Eq. (9.55) and Claim 18 of Ref.
[
42
]
(see also the modification of Claim 18 to strong approximate designs in the proof of Lemma
25
), we have

\left\lVert\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W, \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }-\mathsf{cQ}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{70}(t-1)}{N^{1/8}}+2t^{1/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}.

(C.40)

This yields,

\begin{split}&\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W, \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\leq\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}+\frac{\sqrt{70}(t-1)}{N^{1/8}}+2t^{1/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}.\end{split}

(C.41)

The latter state norm can be written out explicitly, as

\begin{split}&\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\sqrt{\bra*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }\cdot A_{t}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}^{\dagger}\cdot\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }}.\end{split}

(C.42)

where we used that
A_{t}
and
\mathsf{cQ}
act on distinct registers to commute them past one another.

To proceed, we first apply the operator inequality,

1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\preceq\sum_{\ell}\sum_{i\in[\ell]}\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{>}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{>},i}}^{(\ell)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{<}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{<},i}}^{(\ell)}}+\sum_{r}\sum_{j\in[r]}\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{>}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{>},j}}^{(r)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{<}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{<},j}}^{(r)}},

(C.43)

where
\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{>}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{>},i}}^{(\ell)}}
projects onto states with the same bitstring on
\mathsf{A}_{>}
as on
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{>},i}}^{(\ell)}
, and

\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{<}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{<},i}}^{(\ell)}}\equiv 1-\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{<}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{<},i}}^{(\ell)}}

(C.44)

does the reverse on
<
.
For each individual term with
i\in[\ell]
, we have

\begin{split}&\bra*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }\cdot A_{t}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{>}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{>},i}}^{(\ell)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{<}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{<},i}}^{(\ell)}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_t }\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\bra*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }\cdot A_{t}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{<},i}}^{(\ell)}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{>}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{>},i}}^{(\ell)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{<}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{<},i}}^{(\ell)}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{<},i}}^{(\ell)}}}}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} },\end{split}

(C.45)

where all but one of the Clifford unitaries in
\mathsf{cQ}
cancel, since the middle term in the expectation value acts only on register
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{>},i}}
.
For terms with
j\in[r]
, we have instead

\begin{split}&\bra*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }\cdot A_{t}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{>}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{>},j}}^{(r)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{<}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{<},j}}^{(r)}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\cdot A_{t}\r{a}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\bra*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }\cdot A_{t}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{c}\overline{\mathsf{C}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{<},j}}^{(r)}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{>}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{>},j}}^{(r)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{<}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{<},j}}^{(r)}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot\mathsf{c}\overline{\mathsf{C}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{<},j}}^{(r)}}}}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }.\end{split}

(C.46)

We can upper bound the latter expectation values by performing the twirl over
C
.
From Eq. (
B.23
) and Eq. (
B.24
), this yields an upper bound of
1/N^{1/2}+\varepsilon
on both Eq. (
C.45
) and Eq. (
C.46
).
Therefore, in total, we have an upper bound

\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{(\ell+r)^{2}(1/N^{1/2}+\varepsilon)}\leq t/N^{1/4}+t\varepsilon^{1/2}.

(C.47)

The third term in Eq. (
C.39
) is simpler to bound.
The input state to
W
lies in the subspace
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}_{\leq t}
by construction.
Therefore, the output of
W
lies in the subspace
\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}_{\leq t})}
.
This latter subspace is spanned by two classes of states.
The first class is,

\ket{ y }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}},

(C.48)

for any
\ell+r\leq t
, where
\mathsf{Dom}_{>}(L\cup R)
is distinct,
\mathsf{Im}_{<}(L\cup R)
is distinct, and
y_{<}\notin\mathsf{Im}_{<}(L\cup R)
.
These arise if the
W^{R,\dagger}
branch of
W
is applied.
The second class is,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\ell-r}}\sum_{y\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)}\ket{ y }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L \cup( x , y ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}},

(C.49)

for
\ell+r\leq t
, where
\mathsf{Dom}_{>}(L\cup R)
is distinct,
\mathsf{Im}_{<}(L\cup R)
is distinct, and
x_{>}\notin\mathsf{Dom}_{>}(L\cup R)
.
These arise if the
W^{L}
branch of
W
is applied.
The states above are mutually orthogonal to one another as well as between different
\ell,r
.

The first class of states is invariant under
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}
.
Thus, the projector
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}
acts trivially and incurs no error.
Meanwhile, on the second class of states, we have

\begin{split}&\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\ell-r}}\sum_{y\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)}\ket{ y }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L \cup( x , y ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\ell-r}}\sum_{y_{<}\notin\mathsf{Im}_{<}(L\cup R)}\ket{ y }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L \cup( x , y ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}.\end{split}

(C.50)

The final state is orthogonal to the first class of states, as well as between different
\ell,r
.
The state has norm
(N^{1/2}(N^{1/2}-\ell-r))/(N-\ell-r)\geq 1-t/N^{1/2}
.
The above analysis establishes that
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}_{\leq t})}
is block diagonal between the two classes of input and output states, as well as between different
\ell,r
.
Therefore, the desired error is given by the maximum error within each block.
From the above, the maximum is achieved at
\ell+r=t
, which yields,

\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\big)\cdot W\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{\overline{W}, \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{t/N^{1/2}}.

(C.51)

In total, we have shown that the error in Eq. (
C.39
) is upper bounded by,

\mathsf{TD}_{t}\leq\mathsf{TD}_{t-1}+\frac{2\sqrt{70}(t-1)}{N^{1/8}}+4t^{1/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}+2t/N^{1/4}+2\sqrt{t/N^{1/2}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{70}t(t-1)}{N^{1/8}}+4t^{5/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}+\frac{2t(t+1)}{N^{1/4}},\\

applying the inductive hypothesis.
This completes our proof.
∎

C.3.2
Projected
W
is indistinguishable from projected
\mathsf{lrfO}

We will now show that the path-recording oracle
W
and the LRF oracle
\mathsf{lrfO}
are equal on the locally distinct subspace.
To show this, let us adopt the general notation,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LRF}}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi\cdot\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LRF}}

(C.52)

for any projector
\Pi
on
\mathsf{ALR}
.
We will also let
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{lrfO}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}})}
denote the projector onto the subspace spanned by states of the form Eq. (
C.19
),
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{lrfO}^{*}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}})}
analogously for Eq. (
C.20
),
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}})}
for Eq. (
C.21
), and
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{lrfO}^{T}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}})}
for Eq. (
C.22
).
As indicated in the notation, these project onto the image of the
\mathsf{lrfO}
oracles when they are applied to locally distinct states.

Leveraging these projectors, we can define projected versions of the
\mathsf{lrfO}
oracle as follows,

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\mathsf{lrfO}\cdot\left(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}+\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(W_{R}^{\dagger})}\right)\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}}

(C.53)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger}\cdot\left(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}+\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{lrfO}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(W_{L}^{\dagger})}\right)\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}}

(C.54)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{lrfO}^{*})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\mathsf{lrfO}^{*}\cdot\left(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}+\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{lrfO}^{T}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}_{R}^{\dagger})}\right)\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}}

(C.55)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{lrfO}^{T})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\mathsf{lrfO}^{T}\cdot\left(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}+\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{lrfO}^{*}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}_{L}^{\dagger})}\right)\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}}.

(C.56)

The first key result of this section is that
W^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
are nearly equal up to the compress isometry.

Lemma 28

(
W^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
are nearly equal up to isometry)
.

We have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\leq t}\left(W^{\prime}-\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LRF}}\cdot\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{LRF}}\right)\Pi_{\leq t}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq t/N,

(C.57)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\leq t}\left((W^{\dagger})^{\prime}-\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LRF}}\cdot(\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger})^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{LRF}}\right)\Pi_{\leq t}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq t/N

(C.58)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\leq t}\left(\overline{W}^{\prime}-\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LRF}}\cdot(\mathsf{lrfO}^{*})^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{LRF}}\right)\Pi_{\leq t}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq t/N

(C.59)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\leq t}\left((\overline{W}^{\dagger})^{\prime}-\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LRF}}\cdot(\mathsf{lrfO}^{T})^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{LRF}}\right)\Pi_{\leq t}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq t/N.

(C.60)

Proof.

We focus on the first equality without loss of generality. The remaining three equalities following by symmetric arguments.
The
W^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
oracles act on states in two domains, corresponding to the two terms in parentheses in Eq. (
C.53
).
The first is the domain of
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}}
.
For states in this domain,
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
acts as

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L,R}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{y\in[N]}\delta_{y_{<}\notin\mathsf{Im}_{<}(L\cup R)}\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L \cup\{(x, y)\}, R}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}},

(C.61)

where
(L,R)\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\text{lcdist}}
and
x_{>}\notin\mathsf{Dom}_{>}(L\cup R)
. Meanwhile, on the un-compressed versions of the same states,
W^{\prime}
acts as

\displaystyle W^{\prime}\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{L}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket*{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\ell-r}}\sum_{y\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)}\delta_{y_{<}\notin\mathsf{Im}_{<}(L\cup R)}\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{L \cup\{(x, y)\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}},

(C.62)

After compression by
\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LRF}}
, the actions of the two oracles are identical aside from a normalization ratio of
\sqrt{1-(\ell+r)/N}
.

The second is the domain of
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(W_{R}^{\dagger})}\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}}
.
This domain is spanned by states of the form,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)}\delta_{x_{>}\notin\mathsf{Dom}_{>}(L\cup R)}\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L, R \cup\{(x, y)\}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}},

(C.63)

where
(L,R)\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\text{lcdist}}
and
y_{<}\notin\mathsf{Im}_{<}(L\cup R)
, and we leave the state un-normalized for brevity.
For states in this domain,
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
acts as

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}\sum_{x\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)}

\displaystyle\delta_{x_{>}\notin\mathsf{Dom}_{>}(L\cup R)}\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L, R \cup\{(x, y)\}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}}

(C.64)

\displaystyle=N^{1/2}\frac{N-\ell-r}{N}\frac{N^{1/2}(N^{1/2}-\ell-r)}{N-\ell-r}\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{\mathsf{lrf}_{L, R}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}},

(C.65)

where the second ratio arises from the action of
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(W_{R}^{\dagger})}
, the first ratio arises from the action of
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}})}
, and then factor of
N^{1/2}
arises from applying Eq. (
C.21
).
Meanwhile, on the un-compressed versions of the same states,
W^{\prime}
acts as

\displaystyle W^{\prime}\sum_{x\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)}

\displaystyle\delta_{x_{>}\notin\mathsf{Dom}_{>}(L\cup R)}\ket*{x}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{L}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}}\ket*{R \cup\{(x, y)\}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}}

(C.66)

\displaystyle=(N-\ell-r)^{1/2}\frac{N^{1/2}(N^{1/2}-\ell-r)}{N-\ell-r}\ket*{y}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket*{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}},

(C.67)

After compression by
\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LRF}}
, the actions of the two oracles are identical aside from a normalization ratio of
\sqrt{1-(\ell+r)/N}
.

The
W^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
oracles are block-diagonal between different values of
\ell,r
. Hence, the spectral norm of the difference between the two operators (after compressing
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
) is bounded by the maximum spectral norm of the difference for each
\ell,r
.
From the above analysis, the two oracles are related by a constant re-scaling
\sqrt{1-(\ell+r)/N}\geq 1-(\ell+r)/N
within each
\ell,r
.
Hence, the spectral norm of their difference is at most
(\ell+r)/N
. Applying
\ell+r\leq t
completes the proof.
∎

Using Lemma
28
,
we can then show that
W^{\prime}
is indistinguishable from
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
by any adversary.

Lemma 29

(
W^{\prime}
is indistinguishable from
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
)
.

For any
t
-query oracle adversary
\mathcal{A}
, we have

\left\lVert\Tr_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W', \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)-\Tr_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo', \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)\right\rVert_{1}\leq\frac{t(t+1)}{2N}.

(C.68)

Proof.

From Lemma
28
, every application of
W^{\prime}
in
\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W', \mathfrak{D}}_t }
can be replaced by an application of
\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LFR}}\cdot\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{LFR}}
up to total trace norm error
\sum_{s=1}^{t}s/N=t(t+1)/2N
.
Since the
\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{LFR}}
operations act only the
\mathsf{L}
and
\mathsf{R}
registers, they commute with all other objects in
\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W', \mathfrak{D}}_t }
(namely,
\mathsf{cC}
and
\mathsf{cD}
and
A_{s}
).
This implies that all compress operations between adjacent applications of the
\mathsf{lrfO}
oracle cancel one another, leaving only a first application of
\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{LFR}}
before the first query to
\mathsf{lrfO}
and a last application of
\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LFR}}
following the
t
-th application.
The first application acts trivially because
L
and
R
are empty in the initial state.
The final application has no effect since
\mathsf{L}
and
\mathsf{R}
are traced out in the final state.
Hence, all applications of
\mathsf{Compress}_{\mathsf{LFR}}
and
\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{LFR}}
vanish which yields the state
\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo', \mathfrak{D}}_t }
.
∎

C.3.3
Twirled projected
\mathsf{lrfO}
is indistinguishable from twirled
\mathsf{lrfO}

Finally, we can leverage our results thus far to show that the projected oracle
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
is indistinguishable from the original oracle
\mathsf{lrfO}
by any adversary.

Lemma 30

(Twirled
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
is indistinguishable from twirled
\mathsf{lrfO}
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{D}
be any strong approximate unitary 2-design with additive error
\varepsilon
. For any
t
-query oracle adversary
\mathcal{A}
, we have

\left\lVert\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo', \mathfrak{D}}_t }-\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo, \mathfrak{D}}_t }\right\rVert_{1}=\mathcal{O}(t^{2}/N^{1/16})+\mathcal{O}(t^{5/8}\varepsilon^{1/8}).

Proof.

Let us rewrite the projected oracle as follows,

\displaystyle\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\mathsf{lrfO}\cdot\left(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}+\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}})}\right)\cdot\left(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}+\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(W_{R}^{\dagger})}\right)\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathsf{locbij}},

and similar for
(\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger})^{\prime}
,
(\mathsf{lrfO}^{*})^{\prime}
, and
(\mathsf{lrfO}^{T})^{\prime}
.
This decomposition follows from the original definition because
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}
acts on an orthogonal subspace to
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{lrfO}^{\dagger}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}})}
and
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(W_{R}^{\dagger})}
.
This fact also implies that each sum in parentheses above is a projector.
Hence,
\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime}
is equal to the product of
\mathsf{lrfO}
and four projectors.

From Eq. (
B.18
) and the sequential gentle measurement lemma (Lemma
10
), the trace distance of interest is upper bounded as,

\left\lVert\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo', \mathfrak{D}}_t }-\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo, \mathfrak{D}}_t }\right\rVert_{1}\leq 2\left\lVert\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo', \mathfrak{D}}_t }-\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo, \mathfrak{D}}_t }\right\rVert_{2}\leq 8t\sqrt{1-\langle\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime},\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\big|\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime},\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\rangle}.

To bound the normalization on the right hand side, we apply Lemma
29
and Lemma
27
and Lemma
25
and Theorem
10
to find

\left|\langle\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime},\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\big|\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{lrfO}^{\prime},\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\rangle-\E_{U\sim H}\langle\mathcal{A}^{U,\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\big|\mathcal{A}^{U,\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\rangle\right|\leq\frac{t(t+1)}{2N}+\frac{\sqrt{70}t(t-1)}{N^{1/8}}+\frac{2t(t+1)}{N^{1/4}}+\frac{9t(t+2)}{N^{1/8}}+8t^{5/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}.

We have
\langle\mathcal{A}^{U,\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\big|\mathcal{A}^{U,\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\rangle=1
since
U
is unitary.
Hence, the trace distance is bounded above by
8t
multiplied by the square root of the right hand side of the above equation.
The right hand side is
\mathcal{O}(t^{2}/N^{1/8})+\mathcal{O}(t^{5/4}\varepsilon^{1/4})
.
Hence, the trace distance is
\mathcal{O}(t^{2}/N^{1/16})+\mathcal{O}(t^{5/8}\varepsilon^{1/8})
as claimed.
∎

C.4
LRFC is indistinguishable from a Haar-random unitary

We now prove our main result, that the LRFC ensemble is indistinguishable from a Haar-random unitary.
This follows relatively quickly from the results in the previous sections.

Theorem 11

(LRFC is indistinguishable from Haar-random)
.

Let
\mathfrak{D}
be any strong approximate unitary 2-design with additive error
\varepsilon
. For any
t
-query oracle adversary
\mathcal{A}
, we have

\left\lVert\E_{U\sim\text{\emph{LRFC}}}\left(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{U}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{U}_t }\right)-\E_{U\sim H}\left(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{U}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{U}_t }\right)\right\rVert_{1}=\mathcal{O}(t^{2}/N^{1/16})+\mathcal{O}(t^{5/8}\varepsilon^{1/8}).

Proof.

Our proof follows from the results in the preceding subsections in four steps.
At each step, we bound the trace distance between two density matrices.
The density matrices are,

\displaystyle\rho^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\E_{U\sim H}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{U}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{U}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}}\big)

(C.69)

\displaystyle\rho^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\Tr_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W, \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)

(C.70)

\displaystyle\rho^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\Tr_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W', \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)

(C.71)

\displaystyle\rho^{(3)}

\displaystyle=\Tr_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo', \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)

(C.72)

\displaystyle\rho^{(4)}

\displaystyle=\E_{U\sim\text{LRFC}}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{U}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{U}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}}\big)=\Tr_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{\lrfo, \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{1}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{H}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{2}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{F}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)

(C.73)

The first density matrix is the expected output state of an experiment that queries a Haar-random unitary
U
.
The last density matrix is the expected output of an experiment that queries the a random LRFC unitary.
The intermediary density matrices denote the output state of experiments in which the action of each unitary is replaced with a path-recording oracle from the previous sections.

In the previous sections, we have already bounded the trace distance between each pair of density matrices. These are:

1.

\lVert\rho^{(0)}-\rho^{(1)}\rVert_{1}\leq 18t(t+1)/N^{1/8}
(Lemma
25
and Theorem
10
)

2.

\lVert\rho^{(1)}-\rho^{(2)}\rVert_{1}\leq\sqrt{70}t(t-1)/N^{1/8}+2t(t+1)/N^{1/4}+4t^{5/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}
(Lemma
27
)

3.

\lVert\rho^{(2)}-\rho^{(3)}\rVert_{1}\leq t(t+1)/2N
(Lemma
29
)

4.

\lVert\rho^{(3)}-\rho^{(4)}\rVert_{1}=\mathcal{O}(t^{2}/N^{1/16})+\mathcal{O}(t^{5/8}\varepsilon^{1/8})
(Lemma
30
)

By the triangle inequality, the total trace distance,
\lVert\rho^{(0)}-\rho^{(4)}\rVert_{1}
, is less than the sum of the four distances above.
This yields
\lVert\rho^{(0)}-\rho^{(4)}\rVert_{1}=\mathcal{O}(t^{2}/N^{1/16})
as claimed.
∎

C.5
Proof of Theorem
3
: LRFC is a strong unitary design

We let
\mathfrak{D}
be an exact unitary 2-design
[
100
,
68
]
with additive error zero.
By definition, the output of any quantum experiment that queries any combination of
U
,
U^{\dagger}
,
U^{*}
,
U^{T}
up to
k
times is identical whether
f,h_{1},h_{2}
are
2k
-wise independent random functions versus truly random functions.
From Theorem
11
, the output of any quantum experiment that queries the truly random LRFC ensemble
k
times is close to the output of the same experiment that queries a Haar-random unitary, up to trace distance
\mathcal{O}(k^{2}/N^{1/6})
