where
N=2^{n}
.
Hence, the
2k
-wise independent variant of the LRFC ensemble forms an
\varepsilon
-approximate strong unitary
k
-design with
\varepsilon=\mathcal{O}(t^{2}/N^{1/6})
. ∎

C.6
Proof of Theorem
4
: LRFC is a strong PRU

We let
\mathfrak{D}
be an exact unitary 2-design
[
100
,
68
]
with additive error zero.
By definition, no subexponential-time quantum experiment can distinguish whether
f,h_{1},h_{2}
are PRFs (with security against any subexponential-time quantum adversary) versus truly random functions.
From Theorem
11
, the output of any quantum experiment that queries the truly random LRFC ensemble
k
times is close to the output of the same experiment that queries a Haar-random unitary, up to trace distance
\mathcal{O}(k^{2}/N^{1/6})
where
N=2^{n}
.
This is negligibly small for any
k
subexponential in
n
.
Hence, the pseudorandom variant of the LRFC ensemble forms a strong PRU with security against any subexponential-time quantum adversary. ∎

Appendix D
Gluing strong random unitaries

In this section, we provide a proof of the strong gluing lemma (Lemma
1
).
We then apply the strong gluing lemma to prove our Theorems
5
and
6
on the scrambled two-layer circuit ensemble.

D.1
Proof of Lemma
1
: Gluing strong random unitaries

Our proof is long but straightforward, and uses the path-recording framework introduced in Ref.
[
42
]
.
We refer the reader to Appendices
B
and
C
for a complete introduction to this framework and key notation.
In what follows, we begin in Appendix
D.1.1
by introducing several new objects within the path-recording framework that will be useful in the strong gluing proof.
We then provide a summary of our proof in Appendix
D.1.2
.
Each step in the proof summary is then proven individually in the following Appendices
D.1.3
,
D.1.4
, and
D.1.5
.
At a high-level, our proof follows a roughly similar approach to our analysis of the LRFC ensemble in Appendix
C
.

D.1.1
Preliminaries

In this subsection, we provide a short overview of the new notation used in our proof.

Registers.

Our proof will apply the path-recording framework to the unitaries
U_{\mathsf{abc}}
and
U_{\mathsf{bc}}U_{\mathsf{ab}}
.
To each Haar-random unitary, the path-recording framework associate two ancilla registers.
We denote these registers as
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}
and
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}
,
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}
and
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}
, and
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}
and
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}
, for the three unitaries in consideration.
We denote the system register as
A=\mathsf{a}\cup\mathsf{b}\cup\mathsf{c}
.
As in Ref.
[
42
]
, we also allow an arbitrary-sized physical register
\mathsf{B}
, as well as ancilla registers
\mathsf{C}
and
\mathsf{D}
which purify the twirl over
C,D\sim\mathfrak{D}
.

The registers
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}
,
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}
,
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}
,
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}
,
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}
,
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}
contain relation states.
For
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}
and
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}
, a relation state takes the form,

\displaystyle\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}

\displaystyle=\ket{ \big\{ \big( x^i_\mathsf{a} x^i_\mathsf{b} x^i_\mathsf{c} , y^i_\mathsf{a} y^i_\mathsf{b} y^i_\mathsf{c} \big) : i \in[\ell] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}

(D.1)

\displaystyle\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}

\displaystyle=\ket{ \big\{ \big( x^j_\mathsf{a} x^j_\mathsf{b} x^j_\mathsf{c} , y^j_\mathsf{a} y^j_\mathsf{b} y^j_\mathsf{c} \big) : j \in[r] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},

(D.2)

where
x^{i}_{\alpha}
is bitstring on subsystem
\alpha\in\{\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b},\mathsf{c}\}
(and similar for
y^{i}_{\alpha}
,
x^{j}_{\alpha}
, and
y^{j}_{\alpha}
).
Here,
\ell
and
r
denote the length of the relation state registers.
For
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}
and
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}
and
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}
and
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}
, we write,

\displaystyle\ket{ L_{\mathsf{ab}} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket*{ L_{\mathsf{bc}} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}

\displaystyle=\ket*{ \big\{ \big( x^i_\mathsf{a} x^i_\mathsf{b} , y^i_\mathsf{a} z^i_\mathsf{b} \big) : i \in[\ell] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket*{ \big\{ \big( z'^i_\mathsf{b} x^i_\mathsf{c} , y^i_\mathsf{b} y^i_\mathsf{c} \big) : i \in[\ell'] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}

(D.3)

\displaystyle\ket{ R_{\mathsf{ab}} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket*{ R_{\mathsf{bc}} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}

\displaystyle=\ket*{ \big\{ \big( x^j_\mathsf{a} x^j_\mathsf{b} , y^j_\mathsf{a} z^j_\mathsf{b} \big) : j \in[r] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket*{ \big\{ \big( z'^j_\mathsf{b} x^j_\mathsf{c} , y^j_\mathsf{b} y^j_\mathsf{c} \big) : j \in[r'] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}},

(D.4)

where
z^{i}_{\mathsf{b}}
,
z^{\prime i}_{\mathsf{b}}
,
z^{j}_{\mathsf{b}}
,
z^{\prime j}_{\mathsf{b}}
are bitstrings on subsystem
\mathsf{b}
.
We use a different character,
z
, for these bitstrings, because they will correspond to the bitstrings that appear “between”
U_{\mathsf{bc}}
and
U_{\mathsf{ab}}
in
U_{\mathsf{bc}}U_{\mathsf{ab}}
, and will play a special role in our proof.

Projectors on
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}
and
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}
.

We will often wish to restrict attention to certain subsets of the relation states.
As in Appendix
C
, we have the projector onto bijective relation states,

\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}=\begin{cases}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},&\text{if }\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)\in\mathsf{distinct},\\
&\text{and }\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)\in\mathsf{distinct}\\
0,&\text{else}.\\
\end{cases}

(D.5)

Here,
\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)=\{x^{i}_{\mathsf{a}}x^{i}_{\mathsf{b}}x^{i}_{\mathsf{c}}:i\in[\ell]\}\cup\{x^{j}_{\mathsf{a}}x^{j}_{\mathsf{b}}x^{j}_{\mathsf{c}}:j\in[r]\}
,
and

\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)=\{y^{i}_{\mathsf{a}}y^{i}_{\mathsf{b}}y^{i}_{\mathsf{c}}:i\in[\ell]\}\cup\{y^{j}_{\mathsf{a}}y^{j}_{\mathsf{b}}y^{j}_{\mathsf{c}}:j\in[r]\}
.
Identical to before, we also define the not-in-domain projector on
\mathsf{ALR}
as,

\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}=\begin{cases}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},&\text{if }x_{\mathsf{a}}x_{\mathsf{b}}x_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R),\\
0,&\text{else},\\
\end{cases}

(D.6)

and the not-in-image projector as,

\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Im}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}=\begin{cases}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},&\text{if }y_{\mathsf{a}}y_{\mathsf{b}}y_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R),\\
0,&\text{else}.\\
\end{cases}

(D.7)

For the strong gluing proof, we will also introduce new local variants of the above projectors.
These are extremely similar to those defined in Appendix
C
.
For this reason, we use the same notation here as in Appendix
C
, even though the precise definitions are slightly different.
We define the
locally bijective
relation states via the projector,

\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}=\begin{cases}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},&\text{if }\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}\in\mathsf{distinct},\forall\alpha=\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b},\mathsf{c}\\
&\text{and }\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}\in\mathsf{distinct},\forall\alpha=\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b},\mathsf{c}\\
0,&\text{else},\\
\end{cases}

(D.8)

where
\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}=\{x^{i}_{\alpha}:i\in[\ell]\}\cup\{x^{j}_{\alpha}:j\in[r]\}
,
and

\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)=\{y^{i}_{\alpha}:i\in[\ell]\}\cup\{y^{j}_{\alpha}:j\in[r]\}
.
Similarly, we define the not-in-local-domain and not-in-local-image projectors as,

\displaystyle\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},&\text{if }x_{\alpha}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha},\forall\alpha=\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b},\mathsf{c}\\
0,&\text{else},\\
\end{cases}

(D.9)

\displaystyle\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locIm}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},&\text{if }y_{\alpha}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\alpha},\,\,\forall\alpha=\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b},\mathsf{c}\\
0,&\text{else}.\\
\end{cases}

(D.10)

We also let,

\displaystyle\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}

\displaystyle=1-\Pi^{\mathsf{Dom}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}+\Pi^{\mathsf{locDom}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}

(D.11)

\displaystyle\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Im}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locIm}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}

\displaystyle=1-\Pi^{\mathsf{Im}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}+\Pi^{\mathsf{locIm}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},

(D.12)

denote projectors which do nothing if
x_{\mathsf{a}}x_{\mathsf{b}}x_{\mathsf{c}}\in\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)
, and project to the not-in-local-domain subspace if
x_{\mathsf{a}}x_{\mathsf{b}}x_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)
(and similar for
\mathsf{Im}
).

Projectors on
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}
,
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}
,
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}
,
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}
and the
\mathsf{Compress}
partial isometry.

Let us now turn to the registers
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}
,
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}
,
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}
,
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}
.
A key component of our proof is to construct a partial isometry between relation states on
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}\otimes\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}
and those on
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}\otimes\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}\otimes\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}\otimes\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}
.
Namely, for any state in
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}
on
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}\otimes\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}
, we define an “un-compressed” state,

\begin{split}\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}&\cdot\ket*{ \big\{ \big( x^i_\mathsf{a} x^i_\mathsf{b} x^i_\mathsf{c} , y^i_\mathsf{a} y^i_\mathsf{b} y^i_\mathsf{c} \big) : i \in[\ell] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket*{ \big\{ \big( x^j_\mathsf{a} x^j_\mathsf{b} x^j_\mathsf{c} , y^j_\mathsf{a} y^j_\mathsf{b} y^j_\mathsf{c} \big) : j \in[r] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\\
&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{b}}^{\ell+r}}}\sum_{z_{\ell},z_{r}}\ket*{ \big\{ \big( x^i_\mathsf{a} x^i_\mathsf{b} , y^i_\mathsf{a} z^i_\mathsf{b} \big) : i \in[\ell] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket*{ \big\{ \big( z^i_\mathsf{b} x^i_\mathsf{c} , y^i_\mathsf{b} y^i_\mathsf{c} \big) : i \in[\ell] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\otimes\ket*{ \big\{ \big( x^j_\mathsf{a} x^j_\mathsf{b} , y^j_\mathsf{a} z^j_\mathsf{b} \big) : j \in[r] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket*{ \big\{ \big( z^j_\mathsf{b} x^j_\mathsf{c} , y^j_\mathsf{b} y^j_\mathsf{c} \big) : j \in[r] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}},\end{split}

(D.13)

where we abbreviate
z_{\ell}\equiv\{z^{i}_{\mathsf{b}}:i\in[\ell]\}
,
z_{r}\equiv\{z^{j}_{\mathsf{b}}:j\in[r]\}
.
The final state is a valid relation state because
\{y^{i}_{\mathsf{a}}:i\in[\ell]\}\cup\{y^{j}_{\mathsf{a}}:j\in[r]\}
and
\{x^{i}_{\mathsf{c}}:i\in[\ell]\}\cup\{x^{j}_{\mathsf{c}}:j\in[r]\}
are distinct by assumption.
We define
\mathsf{Compress}
as the adjoint of this operation.

The range of
\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}
on
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}\otimes\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}\otimes\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}\otimes\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}
consists of the
paired
relation states,

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{b}}^{\ell+r}}}\sum_{z_{\ell},z_{r}}&\ket{ L_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_\ell} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_\ell} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_r} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_r} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}\\
&\quad\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{b}}^{\ell+r}}}\sum_{z_{\ell},z_{r}}\ket*{ \big\{ \big( x^i_\mathsf{a} x^i_\mathsf{b} , y^i_\mathsf{a} z^i_\mathsf{b} \big) : i \in[\ell] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket*{ \big\{ \big( z^i_\mathsf{b} x^i_\mathsf{c} , y^i_\mathsf{b} y^i_\mathsf{c} \big) : i \in[\ell] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\otimes\ket*{ \big\{ \big( x^j_\mathsf{a} x^j_\mathsf{b} , y^j_\mathsf{a} z^j_\mathsf{b} \big) : j \in[r] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket*{ \big\{ \big( z^j_\mathsf{b} x^j_\mathsf{c} , y^j_\mathsf{b} y^j_\mathsf{c} \big) : j \in[r] \big\} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}},\\
\end{split}

(D.14)

for any locally bijective
L\equiv\{(x^{i}_{\mathsf{a}}x^{i}_{\mathsf{b}}x^{i}_{\mathsf{c}},y^{i}_{\mathsf{a}}y^{i}_{\mathsf{b}}y^{i}_{\mathsf{c}}):i\in[\ell]\}

and
R\equiv\{(x^{j}_{\mathsf{a}}x^{j}_{\mathsf{b}}x^{j}_{\mathsf{c}},y^{j}_{\mathsf{a}}y^{j}_{\mathsf{b}}y^{j}_{\mathsf{c}}):j\in[r]\}
.
We let
\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}
denote the projector onto the set of states above.
We have

\displaystyle\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}

(D.15)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}},

(D.16)

by construction.

D.1.2
Proof overview

Our proof proceeds in five steps.
At each step, we bound the trace distance between two density matrices.
The density matrices are,

\displaystyle\rho^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\E_{U_{\mathsf{abc}}\sim H}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{U_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{U_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)

(D.17)

\displaystyle\rho^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\Tr_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)

(D.18)

\displaystyle\rho^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\Tr_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)

(D.19)

\displaystyle\rho^{(3)}

\displaystyle=\Tr_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)

(D.20)

\displaystyle\rho^{(4)}

\displaystyle=\Tr_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{bc}} V_{\mathsf{ab}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{bc}} V_{\mathsf{ab}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)

(D.21)

\displaystyle\rho^{(5)}

\displaystyle=\E_{U_{\mathsf{ab}},U_{\mathsf{bc}}\sim H}\big(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{U_{\mathsf{bc}} U_{\mathsf{ab}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{U_{\mathsf{bc}} U_{\mathsf{ab}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{B}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}}\big)

(D.22)

The first density matrix is the expected output state of an experiment that queries a Haar-random unitary
U_{\mathsf{abc}}
(and its inverse, conjugate, and transpose).
The last density matrix is the expected output of an experiment that queries
U_{\mathsf{bc}}U_{\mathsf{ab}}
.

The intermediary density matrices denote the output state of experiments in which the action of each unitary is replaced with a path-recording oracle.
In particular, in the third step,
\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_t}
denotes the state in which each application of
W_{\mathsf{abc}}
,
W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\dagger}
,
\overline{W}_{\mathsf{abc}}
,
\overline{W}_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\dagger}
is replaced by the operators,

\displaystyle W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot W_{\mathsf{abc}}\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}

(D.23)

\displaystyle(W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{abc}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{abc}}\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Im}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locIm}}

(D.24)

\displaystyle\overline{W}_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\overline{W}_{\mathsf{abc}}\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}

(D.25)

\displaystyle(\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{abc}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{abc}}\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Im}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locIm}},

(D.26)

respectively.
Similarly, in the fourth step,
\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_t }
denotes the state in which each application of
W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}}
,
W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{ab}}W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{bc}}
,
\overline{W}_{\mathsf{bc}}\overline{W}_{\mathsf{ab}}
,
\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{ab}}\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{bc}}
is replaced by,

\displaystyle(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}\cdot W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(W_{\mathsf{abc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}

(D.27)

\displaystyle(W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{ab}}W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{bc}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}\cdot W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{ab}}W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{bc}}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{abc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{Im}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locIm}}

(D.28)

\displaystyle(\overline{W}_{\mathsf{bc}}\overline{W}_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}\cdot\overline{W}_{\mathsf{bc}}\overline{W}_{\mathsf{ab}}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}_{\mathsf{abc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}

(D.29)

\displaystyle(\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{ab}}\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{bc}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}\cdot\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{ab}}\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{bc}}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{abc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{Im}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locIm}},

(D.30)

respectively. Here, we let
\widetilde{\Pi}\equiv\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi\cdot\mathsf{Compress}
, for any projector
\Pi
.

We bound the trace distance between each pair of density matrices as follows.

1.

\lVert\rho^{(0)}-\rho^{(1)}\rVert_{1}\leq 9t(t+2)/N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}+2t^{1/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}
(Theorem
10
and Lemma
25
)

2.

\lVert\rho^{(1)}-\rho^{(2)}\rVert_{1}\leq 17t^{2}/N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}+7t^{3/2}/(\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha})^{1/2}+6t^{5/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}
(Section
D.1.3
)

3.

\lVert\rho^{(2)}-\rho^{(3)}\rVert_{1}\leq t^{2}/N_{\mathsf{ab}}+t^{2}/N_{\mathsf{bc}}
(Section
D.1.4
)

4.

\lVert\rho^{(3)}-\rho^{(4)}\rVert_{1}\leq 2t\sqrt{\frac{17t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}+\frac{7t^{3/2}}{(\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha})^{1/2}}+\frac{2t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{ab}}}+\frac{2t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{bc}}}+\frac{9t}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}+8t^{5/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}}
(Section
D.1.5
)

5.

\lVert\rho^{(4)}-\rho^{(5)}\rVert_{1}\leq 9t(t+1)/N_{\mathsf{ab}}^{1/8}+9t(t+1)/N_{\mathsf{bc}}^{1/8}
(Theorem
10
)

By the triangle inequality, the total trace distance,
\lVert\rho^{(0)}-\rho^{(5)}\rVert_{1}
, is less than the sum of the five distances above.
If each local Hilbert space has dimension at least
N_{\alpha}\geq 2^{\xi}
, then we have
\lVert\rho^{(0)}-\rho^{(5)}\rVert_{1}\leq\mathcal{O}\big(t^{2}/2^{(3/16)\xi}\big)+\mathcal{O}\big(t^{5/8}\varepsilon^{1/8}\big)
as claimed. ∎

D.1.3
Twirled
W_{\mathsf{abc}}
is indistinguishable from twirled projected
W_{\mathsf{abc}}

We will prove that

\mathsf{TD}_{t}\equiv\mathsf{TD}\left(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t },\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_t }\right)\leq\frac{2\sqrt{70}t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}+\frac{4\sqrt{3}t^{3/2}}{(\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha})^{1/2}}+6t^{5/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}.

(D.31)

This implies that
\lVert\rho^{(1)}-\rho^{(2)}\rVert_{1}
is less than the same value since the 1-norm cannot increase after tracing out
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}
.
The claim follows since
2\sqrt{70}<17
and
4\sqrt{3}<7
.

We proceed by induction.
The statement holds trivially at
t=0
.
To prove the inductive step, suppose that the Eq. (
D.31
) holds up to time
t-1
for any
t\geq 1
.
Without loss of generality, we assume that the forward unitary is applied at time
t
.
The case when the inverse or conjugate or transpose are applied follow by symmetric arguments.
The states at time
t
are obtained from the states at time
t-1
as follows,

\displaystyle\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_{t} }

\displaystyle=\mathsf{cD}\cdot W_{\mathsf{abc}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }

(D.32)

\displaystyle\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_{t} }

\displaystyle=\mathsf{cD}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot W_{\mathsf{abc}}\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }

(D.33)

We have

\begin{split}\mathsf{TD}_{t}\leq\mathsf{TD}_{t}&+2\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\\
&\quad+2\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\big)\cdot W_{\mathsf{abc}}\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2},\\
\end{split}

(D.34)

where the first term accounts for the error up to time
t
, the second term for the error induced by the projector
\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}
[using Eq. (
B.18
)], and the third term for the error induced by the projector
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}
[again using Eq. (
B.18
)].

We bound the second term as follows.
From Eq. (9.55) and Claim 18 of Ref.
[
42
]
(see also the modification of Claim 18 to strong approximate designs in the proof of Lemma
25
), we have

\left\lVert\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }-\mathsf{cQ}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{70}t}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}+2t^{1/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}.

(D.35)

This yields,

\begin{split}&\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\leq\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}+\frac{\sqrt{70}t}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}++2t^{1/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}.\end{split}

(D.36)

The latter state norm can be written out explicitly, as

\begin{split}&\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\sqrt{\bra*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }\cdot A_{t}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}^{\dagger}\cdot\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }}.\end{split}

(D.37)

where we used that
A_{t}
and
\mathsf{cQ}
act on distinct registers to commute them past one another.

To proceed, we first apply the operator inequality,

1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\preceq\sum_{\alpha}\sum_{i\in[\ell]}\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\alpha}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\alpha},i}}^{(\ell)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\bar{\alpha}}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},i}}^{(\ell)}}+\sum_{\alpha}\sum_{j\in[r]}\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{\alpha},j}}^{(r)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\bar{\alpha}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},j}}^{(r)}},

(D.38)

where
\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\alpha}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\alpha},i}}^{(\ell)}}
projects onto states with the same bitstring on
\mathsf{A}_{\alpha}
as on
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},i}}^{(\ell)}
, and

\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\bar{\alpha}}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},i}}^{(\ell)}}\equiv 1-\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\bar{\alpha}}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},i}}^{(\ell)}}

(D.39)

does the reverse on
\bar{\alpha}
(where we define
\bar{\alpha}\equiv\mathsf{bc}
if
\alpha=\mathsf{a}
, and analogous for
\alpha=\mathsf{b},\mathsf{c}
).
For each individual term with
i\in[\ell]
, we have

\begin{split}&\bra*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }\cdot A_{t}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\alpha}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\alpha},i}}^{(\ell)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\bar{\alpha}}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},i}}^{(\ell)}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\bra*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }\cdot A_{t}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},i}}^{(\ell)}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\alpha}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\alpha},i}}^{(\ell)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\bar{\alpha}}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},i}}^{(\ell)}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},i}}^{(\ell)}}}}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} },\end{split}

(D.40)

where all but one of the Clifford unitaries in
\mathsf{cQ}
cancel, since the middle term in the expectation value acts only on register
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{X_{\alpha},i}}
.
For terms with
j\in[r]
, we have instead

\begin{split}&\bra*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }\cdot A_{t}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{\alpha},j}}^{(r)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\bar{\alpha}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},j}}^{(r)}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{D}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\bra*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }\cdot A_{t}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{c}\overline{\mathsf{C}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},j}}^{(r)}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\alpha}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{\alpha},j}}^{(r)}}\Pi^{\mathsf{neq}}_{\mathsf{A}_{\bar{\alpha}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},j}}^{(r)}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\cdot\mathsf{c}\overline{\mathsf{C}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{C}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{\bar{\alpha}},j}}^{(r)}}}}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }.\end{split}

(D.41)

We can upper bound the latter expectation values by performing the twirl over
C
.
From Eq. (
B.21
) and Eq. (
B.22
), this yields an upper bound of
1/N_{\alpha}
on both Eq. (
D.40
) and Eq. (
D.41
).
Therefore, in total, we have an upper bound

\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\big)\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{(\ell+r)\sum_{\alpha}(1/N_{\alpha}+\varepsilon)}\leq\sqrt{3t/\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha}+3t\varepsilon}.

The third term in Eq. (
D.34
) is simpler to bound.
The input state to
W_{\mathsf{abc}}
lies in the subspace
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}_{\leq t}
by construction.
Therefore, the output of
W_{\mathsf{abc}}
lies in the subspace
\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W_{\mathsf{abc}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}_{\leq t})}
.
This latter subspace is spanned by two classes of states.
The first class is,

\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},

(D.42)

for any
\ell+r\leq t
, where
\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct,
\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct, and
y_{\alpha}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
.
These arise if the
W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{R,\dagger}
branch of
W_{\mathsf{abc}}
is applied.
The second class is,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{abc}}-\ell-r}}\sum_{y_{\mathsf{a}}y_{\mathsf{b}}y_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},

(D.43)

for
\ell+r\leq t
, where
\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct,
\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct, and
x_{\alpha}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
.
These arise if the
W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{L}
branch of
W_{\mathsf{abc}}
is applied.
The states above are mutually orthogonal to one another as well as between different
\ell,r
.

The first class of states is invariant under
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}
.
Thus, the projector
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}
acts trivially and incurs no error.
Meanwhile, on the second class of states, we have

\begin{split}&\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{abc}}-\ell-r}}\sum_{y_{\mathsf{a}}y_{\mathsf{b}}y_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{abc}}-\ell-r}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{\mathsf{a}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{a}}\\
y_{\mathsf{b}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{b}}\\
y_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{c}}\end{subarray}}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}.\end{split}

(D.44)

The final state is orthogonal to the first class of states, as well as between different
\ell,r
.
The state has norm
(\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r))/(N-\ell-r)\geq 1-3t/\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha}
.
The above analysis establishes that
\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W_{\mathsf{abc}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}_{\leq t})}
is block diagonal between the two classes of input and output states, as well as between different
\ell,r
.
Therefore, the desired error is given by the maximum error within each block.
From the above, the maximum is achieved at
\ell+r=t
, which yields,

\left\lVert\big(1-\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\big)\cdot W_{\mathsf{abc}}\cdot\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{3t/\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha}}.

(D.45)

In total, we have shown that the error in Eq. (
D.34
) is upper bounded by,

\mathsf{TD}_{t}\leq\mathsf{TD}_{t}+\frac{2\sqrt{70}t}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}+2t^{1/4}\varepsilon^{1/4}+4\sqrt{\frac{3t}{\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha}}}+2\sqrt{3t\varepsilon}\leq\frac{2\sqrt{70}t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}+\frac{4\sqrt{3}t^{3/2}}{(\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha})^{1/2}}+6t^{5/4}\varepsilon^{1/4},\\

(D.46)

applying the inductive hypothesis.
This completes our proof.

D.1.4
Projected
W_{\mathsf{abc}}
is indistinguishable from projected
W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}}

We will prove that

\left\lVert\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_t }-\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_t }\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{t(t-1)}{2N_{\mathsf{ab}}}+\frac{t(t-1)}{2N_{\mathsf{bc}}}

(D.47)

Using Eq. (
B.18
), this implies that

\mathsf{TD}\left(\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_t }\!\mathsf{Compress},\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_t }\right)\leq\frac{t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{ab}}}+\frac{t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{bc}}}

(D.48)

which implies that
\lVert\rho^{(2)}-\rho^{(3)}\rVert_{1}
is less than the same value since the 1-norm cannot increase after tracing out
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}\mathsf{C}\mathsf{D}
.

We proceed by induction.
The statement holds trivially at
t=0
.
For the inductive step, we assume that Eq. (
D.47
) holds up to time
t-1
.
We will show that the claim holds for time
t
as well.
Without loss of generality, we assume that the forward unitary is applied at time
t
.
The case when the inverse or conjugate or transpose are applied follow by symmetric arguments.

The states at time
t
are obtained from the states at time
t-1
as follows,

\displaystyle\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_{t} }

\displaystyle=\mathsf{cD}\cdot W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }

(D.49)

\displaystyle\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_{t} }

\displaystyle=\mathsf{cD}\cdot(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }

(D.50)

The final projection in
W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\prime}
and
(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime}
guarantees that the input state to the
t
-th application of
W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\prime}
and
(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime}
obeys,

\displaystyle\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }

\displaystyle=\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }

(D.51)

\displaystyle\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }

\displaystyle=\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{t}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }.

(D.52)

We will now analyze the action of first
W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\prime}
and then
(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime}
.

The domain of
W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\prime}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}
contains two classes of states, corresponding to the domain of

\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\prime})}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}=\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W_{\mathsf{abc}})}\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}=\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}+\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W^{R}_{\mathsf{abc}})}\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}.

(D.53)

The second equality follows from the domain of
W_{\mathsf{abc}}
,

\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W_{\mathsf{abc}})}=\Pi^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}}\Pi^{\mathsf{bij}}+\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W^{R}_{\mathsf{abc}})}.

(D.54)

We will consider the action on each class of states separately.
Focusing on the
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}
registers, a complete basis for the first class of states is given by,

\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},

(D.55)

where
\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct,
\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct, and
x_{\alpha}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
.
We then have,

\begin{split}W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\prime}&\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}=\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}W_{\mathsf{abc}}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\\
&=\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{abc}}-\ell-r}}\sum_{y_{\mathsf{a}}y_{\mathsf{b}}y_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\\
&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{abc}}-\ell-r}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{\mathsf{a}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{a}}\\
y_{\mathsf{b}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{b}}\\
y_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{c}}\end{subarray}}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}.\\
\end{split}

(D.56)

Meanwhile, a complete basis for the second class of states is given by

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathsf{a}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{a}}\\
x_{\mathsf{b}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{b}}\\
x_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{c}}\end{subarray}}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},

(D.57)

where
\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct,
\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct, and
y_{\alpha}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
.
We then have,

\begin{split}&W_{\mathsf{abc}}^{\prime}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathsf{a}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{a}}\\
x_{\mathsf{b}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{b}}\\
x_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{c}}\end{subarray}}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\\
&\,\,\,\,=\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}W^{R,\dagger}_{\mathsf{abc}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathsf{a}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{a}}\\
x_{\mathsf{b}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{b}}\\
x_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{c}}\end{subarray}}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\\
&\,\,\,\,=\left(\frac{\sqrt{\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r)}}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{abc}}-\ell-r}}\right)\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\\
&\,\,\,\,=\left(\frac{\sqrt{\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r)}}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{abc}}-\ell-r}}\right)\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}}\ket{ R }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{abc}}}}},\\
\end{split}

(D.58)

where the factor in parentheses arises from the decrease in normalization when the projector
\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W^{R}_{\mathsf{abc}})}
is applied (through
W^{R,\dagger}_{\mathsf{abc}}=W^{R,\dagger}_{\mathsf{abc}}\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W^{R}_{\mathsf{abc}})}
).

Let us now turn to
(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime}
.
The input state to
W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}}
is contained within the domain of

\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(W_{\mathsf{abc}})}\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}=\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{locDom}}\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}+\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{I}(W^{R}_{\mathsf{abc}})}\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}.

(D.59)

As before, we must consider two classes of input states, corresponding to the domain of each term on the right side above.
Focusing on the
\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}
registers, a complete basis for the first class of states is given by,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{b}}^{\ell+r}}}\sum_{z_{\ell},z_{r}}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_\ell} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_\ell} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_r} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_r} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}},

(D.60)

where
\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct,
\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct, and
x_{\alpha}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
.
(We refer to Section
D.1.1
for the definitions of
L_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_{\ell}}
,
L_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_{\ell}}
,
R_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_{r}}
,
R_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_{r}}
,
L
,
R
.)
Focusing on the
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}
registers, the application of
W_{\mathsf{ab}}
gives,

\begin{split}W_{\mathsf{ab}}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}&\ket{L^{z_\ell}_{\mathsf{ab}} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket{R^{z_r}_{\mathsf{ab}} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\\
&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{ab}}-\ell-r}}\sum_{y_{\mathsf{a}}z_{\mathsf{b}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L_{\mathsf{ab}}\cup R_{\mathsf{ab}})}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} z_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{L^{z_\ell}_{\mathsf{ab}} \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} , y_\mathsf{a} z_\mathsf{b} ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}\ket{R^{z_r}_{\mathsf{ab}} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}}.\end{split}

(D.61)

Focusing on the
\mathsf{A}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}
registers, the ensuing application of
W_{\mathsf{bc}}
gives,

\begin{split}W_{\mathsf{bc}}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} z_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}&\ket{L^{z_\ell}_{\mathsf{bc}} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}\ket{R^{z_r}_{\mathsf{bc}} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}\\
&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{\mathsf{bc}}-\ell-r}}\sum_{y_{\mathsf{b}}y_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L_{\mathsf{bc}}\cup R_{\mathsf{bc}})}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}}\ket{L^{z_\ell}_{\mathsf{bc}} \cup( z_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}\ket{R^{z_r}_{\mathsf{bc}} }_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}}.\end{split}

(D.62)

In total, we have the state,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{1}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{\ell},z_{r}\\
y_{\mathsf{a}}z_{\mathsf{b}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L_{\mathsf{ab}}\cup R_{\mathsf{ab}})\\
y_{\mathsf{b}}y_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L_{\mathsf{bc}}\cup R_{\mathsf{bc}})\end{subarray}}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_\ell} \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} , y_\mathsf{a} z_\mathsf{b} )}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_\ell} \cup( z_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_r} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_r} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}},

(D.63)

where
\mathcal{N}_{1}\equiv N_{\mathsf{b}}^{\ell+r}(N_{\mathsf{ab}}-\ell-r)(N_{\mathsf{bc}}-\ell-r)
.
Applying the final projection
\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}
forces the
y_{\alpha}
to be locally distinct, which yields,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{1}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{\ell},z_{r},z_{\mathsf{b}}\\
y_{\mathsf{a}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{a}}\\
y_{\mathsf{b}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{b}}\\
y_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{c}}\end{subarray}}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_\ell} \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} , y_\mathsf{a} z_\mathsf{b} )}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_\ell} \cup( z_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_r} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_r} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}},

(D.64)

where the sum over
z_{\mathsf{b}}
is unrestricted, since
y_{\mathsf{a}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{a}}
implies that
y_{\mathsf{a}}z_{\mathsf{b}}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L_{\mathsf{ab}}\cup R_{\mathsf{ab}})
.
Applying
\mathsf{Compress}
to the state yields the state in Eq. (
D.56
) up to a normalization difference,

\left|\sqrt{\frac{N_{\mathsf{b}}\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r)}{(N_{\mathsf{ab}}-\ell-r)(N_{\mathsf{bc}}-\ell-r)}}-\sqrt{\frac{\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r)}{N-\ell-r}}\right|\leq\frac{\ell+r}{N_{\mathsf{ab}}}+\frac{\ell+r}{N_{\mathsf{bc}}},

(D.65)

where the first inequality holds for
(\ell+r)/N_{\mathsf{ab}}+(\ell+r)/N_{\mathsf{bc}}\leq 1/2
.

We now turn to the second class of states.
A complete basis is given by

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{2}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{\ell},z_{r},z_{\mathsf{b}}\\
x_{\mathsf{a}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{a}}\\
x_{\mathsf{b}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{b}}\\
x_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\mathsf{c}}\end{subarray}}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_\ell} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_\ell} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_r} \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} , y_\mathsf{a} z_\mathsf{b} ) }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_r} \cup( z_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}},

(D.66)

where
\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct,
\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
is distinct,
y_{\alpha}\notin\mathsf{Im}(L\cup R)_{\alpha}
, and the normalization is given by
\mathcal{N}_{2}\equiv N_{\mathsf{b}}^{\ell+r+1}\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r)
.
The application of
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{I}(W^{R}_{\mathsf{abc}})}
gives,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{N}_{3}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{\ell},z_{r},z_{\mathsf{b}}\\
x_{\mathsf{a}}x_{\mathsf{b}}x_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\ket{ x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_\ell} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_\ell} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_r} \cup( x_\mathsf{a} x_\mathsf{b} , y_\mathsf{a} z_\mathsf{b} ) }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_r} \cup( z_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}},

(D.67)

where
\mathcal{N}_{3}=N_{\mathsf{b}}^{\ell+r+1}(N_{\mathsf{abc}}-\ell-r)^{2}/\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r)
.
The application of
W_{\mathsf{ab}}
gives,

\sqrt{\frac{N_{\mathsf{ab}}-\ell-r}{\mathcal{N}_{3}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{\ell},z_{r},z_{\mathsf{b}}\\
x_{\mathsf{c}}\end{subarray}}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} z_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_\ell} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_\ell} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_r} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_r} \cup( z_\mathsf{b} x_\mathsf{c} , y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} ) }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}},

(D.68)

where the sum over
x_{\mathsf{c}}
is unconstrained because the application of
W^{R,\dagger}_{\mathsf{ab}}
enforces that
x_{\mathsf{a}}x_{\mathsf{b}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L_{\mathsf{ab}}\cup R_{\mathsf{ab}})
, which implies that
x_{\mathsf{a}}x_{\mathsf{b}}x_{\mathsf{c}}\notin\mathsf{Dom}(L\cup R)
.
The application of
W_{\mathsf{bc}}
then gives,

\sqrt{\frac{(N_{\mathsf{ab}}-\ell-r)(N_{\mathsf{bc}}-\ell-r)}{\mathcal{N}_{3}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{\ell},z_{r}\end{subarray}}\ket{ y_\mathsf{a} y_\mathsf{b} y_\mathsf{c} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_\ell} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ L_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_\ell} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{L}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{ab}}^{z_r} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{ab}}}}\ket{ R_{\mathsf{bc}}^{z_r} }_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bc}}}}.

(D.69)

The state is invariant under the final projection
\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}
.
Applying
\mathsf{Compress}
to the state yields the state in Eq. (
D.58
) up to a normalization difference,

\left|\sqrt{\frac{(N_{\mathsf{ab}}-\ell-r)(N_{\mathsf{bc}}-\ell-r)\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r)}{N_{\mathsf{b}}(N-\ell-r)^{2}}}-\sqrt{\frac{\prod_{\alpha}(N_{\alpha}-\ell-r)}{(N_{\mathsf{abc}}-\ell-r)}}\right|\leq\frac{\ell+r}{N},

(D.70)

where the first inequality holds for
\ell+r\leq 2N_{\mathsf{abc}}
.

Note that both
\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\overline{W_{\mathsf{abc}}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}
and
\overline{W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}
are block diagonal in
\ell
and
r
, as well as between the two classes of states considered in the above analysis.
Thus, the spectral norm of the difference of the two operators is given by the maximum spectral norm of the difference within each block.
From the above analysis, the maximum is achieved for the first class of states, at
\ell+r=t-1
. The spectral norm is thus bounded by

\left\lVert\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\overline{W_{\mathsf{abc}}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\Pi^{\leq t-1}-\overline{W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}\cdot\Pi^{\leq t-1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{(t-1)}{N_{\mathsf{ab}}}+\frac{(t-1)}{N_{\mathsf{bc}}},

where
\Pi^{\leq t-1}
restricts to relation state register lengths
\ell+r\leq t-1
.
We have,

\begin{split}&\left\lVert\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_{t} }-\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_{t} }\right\rVert_{2}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\leq\left\lVert\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }-\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_{t-1} }\right\rVert_{2}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\left\lVert\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\overline{W_{\mathsf{abc}}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{locbij}}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\Pi^{\leq t-1}-(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime}\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}\cdot\Pi^{\leq t-1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\leq\mathsf{TD}_{t-1}+(t-1)/N_{\mathsf{ab}}+(t-1)/N_{\mathsf{bc}}\\
\end{split}

as claimed. This completes our proof of step 2.

D.1.5
Twirled projected
W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}}
is indistinguishable from twirled
V_{\mathsf{bc}}V_{\mathsf{ab}}

Our proof of step 4 follows quickly from the results of steps 1-3.
We use that
W_{\mathsf{bc}}
and
W_{\mathsf{ab}}
are restrictions of
V_{\mathsf{bc}}
and
V_{\mathsf{ab}}
to write,

\displaystyle(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}\cdot V_{\mathsf{bc}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W_{\mathsf{bc}})}\cdot V_{\mathsf{ab}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(W_{\mathsf{bc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(W_{\mathsf{abc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}

(D.71)

\displaystyle(W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{ab}}W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{bc}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}\cdot V^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{ab}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W_{\mathsf{ab}})}\cdot V^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{bc}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(W_{\mathsf{bc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(W^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{abc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{Im}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locIm}}

(D.72)

\displaystyle(\overline{W}_{\mathsf{bc}}\overline{W}_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}\cdot\overline{V}_{\mathsf{bc}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}_{\mathsf{bc}})}\cdot\overline{V}_{\mathsf{ab}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}_{\mathsf{bc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}_{\mathsf{abc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{Dom}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locDom}}

(D.73)

\displaystyle(\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{ab}}\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{bc}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Pi^{\mathsf{paired}}\cdot\overline{V}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{ab}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W}_{\mathsf{ab}})}\cdot\overline{V}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{bc}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathcal{I}(\overline{W}_{\mathsf{bc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathcal{D}(\overline{W}^{\dagger}_{\mathsf{abc}})}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\notin\mathsf{Im}\rightarrow\notin\mathsf{locIm}}

(D.74)

Therefore, the state
\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_t }
differs from the state
\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{bc}} V_{\mathsf{ab}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }
solely by the insertion of projectors throughout the time evolution.

From Eq. (
B.18
) and the sequential gentle measurement lemma (Lemma
10
), the difference between the two states is bounded as,

\big\lVert\rho^{(3)}-\rho^{(4)}\big\rVert_{1}\leq 2\left\lVert\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_t }-\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{bc}} V_{\mathsf{ab}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }\right\rVert_{2}\leq 2t\sqrt{1-\langle\mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime},\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\big|\mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime},\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\rangle}.

(D.75)

From our proofs of step 2 and step 3, we have that

\begin{split}&\mathsf{TD}\left(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}} W_{\mathsf{ab}})', \mathfrak{D}}_t },\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }\!\mathsf{Compress}\right)\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\leq\frac{17t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}+\frac{7t^{3/2}}{(\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha})^{1/2}}+\frac{2t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{ab}}}+\frac{2t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{bc}}}.\end{split}

(D.76)

Meanwhile, from Lemma 9.3 of Ref.
[
42
]
[see in particular Eqs. (9.58), (9.59)], we have

\mathsf{TD}\left(\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{W_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t },\mathsf{cQ}\cdot\outerproduct*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }\cdot\mathsf{cQ}\right)\leq\frac{9t}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}.

(D.77)

The state
\mathsf{cQ}\cdot\ket*{ \mathcal{A}^{V_{\mathsf{abc}}, \mathfrak{D}}_t }
is obtained from solely unitary time-evolution, and thus has norm one.
Combining the two above equations therefore yields,

\langle\mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime},\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\big|\mathcal{A}^{(W_{\mathsf{bc}}W_{\mathsf{ab}})^{\prime},\mathfrak{D}}_{t}\rangle\geq 1-\left(\frac{17t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}+\frac{7t^{3/2}}{(\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha})^{1/2}}+\frac{2t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{ab}}}+\frac{2t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{bc}}}\right)-\frac{9t}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}

(D.78)

which, from Eq. (
D.75
), implies that

\begin{split}\big\lVert\rho^{(3)}-\rho^{(4)}\big\rVert_{1}&\leq 2t\sqrt{\frac{17t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}+\frac{7t^{3/2}}{(\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha})^{1/2}}+\frac{2t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{ab}}}+\frac{2t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{bc}}}+\frac{9t}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/8}}}\\
&\leq\frac{2\sqrt{17}t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/16}}+\frac{2\sqrt{7}t^{7/4}}{(\min_{\alpha}N_{\alpha})^{1/4}}+\frac{2\sqrt{2}t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{ab}}^{1/2}}+\frac{2\sqrt{2}t^{2}}{N_{\mathsf{bc}}^{1/2}}+\frac{6t^{3/2}}{N_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1/16}},\\
\end{split}

(D.79)

where in the second line we use that the square root is subadditive.
This completes the proof.

D.2
Proof of Theorems
5
and
6

Our proof of Theorems
5
and
6
follow immediately from Lemma
1
.
The extension of Theorem
5
to the blocked fast scrambling circuit follows immediately from Lemma
2
.

Proof of Theorem
5
.

Iterating Lemma
1

m=n/\xi
times, we can replace the two-layer circuit with a Haar-random unitary up to a measurable error
(n/\xi)(\varepsilon/n)+\mathcal{O}(nk^{2}/2^{(3/16)\xi}\xi)
.
The first term is less than
\varepsilon/2
whenever
\xi\geq 2
.
The second term is less than
\varepsilon/2
if
\xi\geq\frac{16}{3}\log_{2}(nk^{2}/\varepsilon)+\mathcal{O}(1)
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Remark.
The proof immediately extends to a modified blocked fast scrambling circuit in Section
4.4
of the main text, in which we replace the exact
n
-qubit unitary 2-designs with blocked fast scrambling circuits composed of small strong
\frac{\varepsilon_{2}}{n}
-approximate unitary 2-designs.
From Lemma
2
, this blocked fast scrambling circuit forms a strong
\varepsilon_{2}
-approximate unitary 2-design when
\xi\geq\log_{2}(5n/\varepsilon_{2})
.
Iterating Lemma
1

m=n/\xi
times, we can replace the two-layer circuit with a Haar-random unitary up to a measurable error
(n/\xi)(\varepsilon/n)+\mathcal{O}(nk^{2}/2^{(3/16)\xi}\xi)+\mathcal{O}(nk^{5/8}\varepsilon_{2}^{1/8})
.
The first term is less than
\varepsilon/3
whenever
\xi\geq 3
.
The second term is less than
\varepsilon/3
if
\xi\geq\frac{16}{3}\log_{2}(nk^{2}/\varepsilon)+\mathcal{O}(1)
.
The third term is less than
\varepsilon/3
if
\varepsilon_{2}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{8}/n^{8}k^{5})
, which requires
\xi\geq\log_{2}(n^{9}k^{5}/\varepsilon^{8})+\mathcal{O}(1)=\mathcal{O}(\log nk/\varepsilon)
. ∎

Proof of Theorem
6
.

By assumption, each individual small PRU in the two-layer circuit is indistinguishable from a small Haar-random unitary by any
\poly n
-time quantum experiment.
Hence, following identical steps to the proof of Theorem 2 in Ref.
[
36
]
, the scrambled two-layer circuit of small PRUs is indistinguishable from a scrambled two-layer circuit of small Haar-random unitaries.
From Theorem
6
, the latter ensemble forms an
\varepsilon
-approximate strong unitary
k
-design for any
k^{2}/\varepsilon\leq\mathcal{O}(2^{\xi}/n)
.
Setting
\xi=\omega(\log n)
yields a design for any
k,1/\varepsilon=\poly n
.
Hence, from the definition of strong approximate unitary
k
-designs, the scrambled two-layer circuit of small Haar-random unitaries is indistinguishable from a Haar-random unitary by any
\poly n
-time quantum experiment.
∎

1
and
2

The combination of Theorem
3
and Theorem
5
immediately yield Theorem
1
on the circuit depth of strong unitary designs.

1
.

The second and third statements of Theorem
1
follows immediately from Theorem
3
and the circuit depth required to implement the LRFC ensemble with
2k
-wise independent functions
[
39
]
.
The first statement of Theorem
1
follows from Theorem
5
and Lemma
3
.
∎

The combination of Theorem
4
and Theorem
6
immediately yield Theorem
2
on the circuit depth of strong pseudorandom unitaries.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

The first statement of Theorem
2
follows immediately from Theorem
4
and the circuit depth required to implement the LRFC ensemble with pseudorandom functions
[
36
]
.
The second statement of Theorem
2
follows from Theorem
4
and Theorem
6
.
The derivation of the circuit depth is described in the main text.
∎

Appendix E
Ancilla-free pseudorandom unitaries

In this section, we give the first constructions of ancilla-free (strong) pseudorandom unitarites. Our main crytographic building block will be pseudorandom functions
[
77
]
computable in the complexity class “logspace-uniform
\mathsf{TC}^{1}
.” A function is computable in logspace-uniform
\mathsf{TC}^{1}
if (1) it is computable by a family of
O(\log n)
-depth circuits with large fan-in threshold gates and (2) this family of circuits is output by a logspace Turing machine on the input
1^{n}
. Crucially, it is known that such PRFs exist under the LWE assumption
[
78
]
, and that this construction is post-quantum secure
[
74
]
.

Our main technical result about ancilla-free computation is as follows.

Theorem 12
.

Let
f:\{0,1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m}
be any logspace-uniform
\mathsf{TC}^{1}
-computable function, where
q=O(1)
. Then, there is a
\mathsf{poly}(n,m)
-size reversible circuit implementing the permutation

(x,y,a)\mapsto(x,y+f(x)\pmod{q},a),

(E.1)

where
a
denotes an arbitrary setting of the ancilla register.

By combining
Theorem

12
with the LRFC construction of
Section

4.1
, obtain the following intermediate result: a PRU family with an efficient ancilla-free implementation of the unitary
U_{k}\otimes\mathsf{Id}
(rather than
U_{k}
alone). We call such PRUs “ancilla-independent.”

Theorem 13
.

Assuming polynomially secure (respectively, sub-exponentially secure) post-quantum PRFs computable in logspace-uniform
\mathsf{TC}^{1}
, there exist polynomially secure (respectively, sub-exponentially secure) ancilla-independent strong PRUs.

Finally, combining
Theorem

14
with the strong PRU gluing lemma (and its corollary,
Theorem

6
), we obtain ancilla-free strong PRUs. This approach works because when gluing ancilla-independent PRU implementations, one can use the ancilla register of one unitary as part of the
input
register of another unitary. In the end, the resulting glued circuit will compute a PRU on its entire domain.

Theorem 14
.

Assuming post-quantum PRFs computable in
\mathsf{TC}^{1}
, there exist ancilla-free strong PRUs. Moreover, assuming sub-exponentially secure post-quantum PRFs computable in
\mathsf{TC}^{1}
, there exist ancilla-free strong PRUs computable in depth
\poly(\log n)
with all-to-all circuits.

Since logspace-uniform
\mathsf{TC}^{1}
-computable PRFs are known under the standard LWE assumption
[
78
]
, we obtain instantiations of our results under LWE.

Corollary 2
.

Assuming the post-quantum hardness of LWE, there exist ancilla-free PRUs. Assuming the sub-exponential post-quantum hardness of LWE, there exist ancilla-free strong PRUs computable in depth
\poly(\log n)
with all-to-all circuits.

The rest of this section is devoted to proving
Theorems

12
,
13
and
14
.

E.1
Ancilla-preserving reversible computation of functions

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{q}
denote a function with
q=O(1)
. We study different “ancilla-respecting” reversible circuit implementations of the computation

\ket{x, y}\mapsto x,y+f(x)\ket{x, y+ f(x) \pmod q}.

(E.2)

We will use bra-ket notation to describe the action of these reversible circuits, but we note that they only use Toffoli gates and thus correspond to classical reversible computation, i.e., permutations.

Our reversible circuits will operate on four registers:

•

Let
\mathsf{X}
denote an
n
-qubit register whose standard basis states correspond to
\mathcal{X}
.

•

Let
\mathsf{Y}
denote an output register with standard basis in bijection with
\mathbb{Z}_{q}
.

•

Let
\mathsf{A}
denote an
\ell
-qubit
trusted
ancilla register. This means that (at least initially), our computations will rely on the ancilla being initialized to the
\ket*{0^\ell}
state.

•

Let
\mathsf{W}
denote an
untrusted
(or catalytic) ancilla register of size
\poly n
.

Definition 43
.

We say that a reversible circuit
C
acting on
(\mathsf{X},\mathsf{Y},\mathsf{A},\mathsf{W})
is an
ancilla-preserving
implementation of
f
with trusted space
\mathsf{A}
and untrusted space
\mathsf{W}
if it maps

\displaystyle\ket{x,y,a,w}\mapsto\ket{x,y + g(x,a,w),a,w},

(E.3)

where
g
is any function that agrees with
f
when
a=0
. That is,
g(x,0^{\ell},w)=f(x)
, but otherwise
g(x,a,w)
may be arbitrary.

Here, “ancilla-preserving” refers to the fact that
C
never changes the values stored in the
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{W}
register, regardless of what they are initialized to. We say that
\mathsf{A}
is “trusted space”, since
g(x,a,w)
is only guaranteed to compute the output
f(x)
correctly when
\mathsf{A}
is initialized properly to
0^{\ell}
.
\mathsf{W}
is “untrusted” because we require that
g(x,0,w)
correctly compute
f(x)
for any choice of
w
.

As we will show, the following lemma is an easy consequence of recent work on logspace catalytic classical computation
[
101
,
79
]
.

Lemma 31
.

For every function
f:\{0,1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{q}
computable in logspace-uniform
\mathsf{TC}^{1}
, there is a
\poly(n)
-size ancilla-preserving reversible circuit
C
that implements
f
with an
\ell=O(\log n)
-size trusted ancilla space
\mathsf{A}
and a
\poly(n)
-size untrusted ancilla space
\mathsf{W}
.

Proof.

According to
[
79
]
, for
f:\{0,1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{q}
computable in logspace-uniform
\mathsf{TC}^{1}
, there is a
\poly(n)
-size reversible circuit
C^{\prime}
that maps

\displaystyle\ket*{x,y,a,w}\mapsto\ket*{p(x,y,a,w)}

(E.4)

where
p(x,0,0^{\ell},w)=(x^{\prime},f(x),a^{\prime},w)
, but on other inputs,
p(x,y,a,w)
may be arbitrary. Given such a circuit
C^{\prime}
, we can implement
C
satisfying
Definition

43
, where the trusted ancilla is now size
\ell+O(1)
, using standard techniques from reversible computation:

1.

On input
\ket{x,y,a,w}
, parse
a
as
(y^{\prime},a^{\prime})
, where
y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}
and
a^{\prime}
is length
\ell
. Run
C^{\prime}
on
(x,y^{\prime},a^{\prime},w)
. This step does not affect the
y
register, and the effect on
\ket{x,a,w}
is:

\displaystyle\ket*{x,a= (y',a'),w}\mapsto\ket*{p(x,y',a',w)}.

(E.5)

2.

Parse
p(x,y^{\prime},a^{\prime},w)
as
p(x,y^{\prime},a^{\prime},w)=(x_{\mathrm{out}},y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{out}},a^{\prime}_{\mathrm{out}},w_{\mathrm{out}})
. Add the value of
y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{out}}
onto
\ket{y}
.

3.

Apply the inverse of
C^{\prime}
.

The result is that we have performed the map

\displaystyle\ket{x,y,a,w}\mapsto\ket*{x,y + y'_{\mathrm{out}},a,w},

(E.6)

where
y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{out}}
is a function of
(x,a=(y^{\prime},a^{\prime}),w)
, which equals
f(x)
when
a=(y^{\prime},a^{\prime})=(0,0^{\ell})
.
∎

We will now define two further restricted classes of ancilla-preserving implementations, and we will show how to compile circuits satisfying
Definition

43
into circuits satisfying these more restricted notions.

Recall that an ancilla-preserving implementation of
f
is a circuit
C
that maps
(x,y,a,w)
to
(x,y+g(x,a,w),a,w)
, where
g
is
any function
that agrees with
f
when
a=0
. We now define two more restrictive notions where we impose further requirements on
g
.

Definition 44

(Stronger versions of ancilla-preserving implementations)
.

Let
C
be an ancilla-preserving implementation of
f
, and let
g
be as in
Definition

43
. Then we say that
C
is:

•

an
ancilla-controlled
implementation of
f
if
g(x,a,w)=f(x)\cdot\chi_{a=0^{\ell}}
and is
0
otherwise.

•

an
ancilla-independent
implementation of
f
if
g(x,a,w)=f(x)
for all
a,w
.

E.1.1
Converting ancilla-preserving to ancilla-controlled

Next, we show that an ancilla-preserving implementation of a function
f
,

\ket{x, y, a, w}\mapsto\ket{x, y + g(x,a,w), a, w},

can be efficiently converted into an ancilla-controlled implementation of
f
,

\ket{x, y, a, w'}\mapsto\ket{x,y + \chi_{a = 0^\ell}\cdot f(x), a, w'},

where the trusted ancilla space
\mathcal{A}
is unchanged and
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}=\mathcal{W}\times\mathbb{Z}_{q}
.

Lemma 32
.

Let
g(x,a,w)
be any function such that
g(x,0,w)=f(x)
for all
(x,w)
. Then, if there is a circuit
C
implementing the map
\ket{x, y, a, w}\mapsto\ket{x, y + g(x,a,w), a, w}
, there is another circuit
C^{\prime}
implementing the map
\ket{x, y, a, w'}\mapsto\ket{x,y + \chi_{a = 0^\ell}\cdot f(x), a, w'}
.

Moreover, the size and depth of
C^{\prime}
is bounded in terms of
C
:

•

|C^{\prime}|=O(|C|)+2^{O(\ell)}
,
9
9

9

We believe it should be possible to improve the
2^{O(\ell)}
dependence to
\poly(\ell)
, but due to a subsequent
2^{O(\ell)}
overhead, it makes no difference for our purposes.
and

•

\mathrm{depth}(C^{\prime})=O(\mathrm{depth}(C))+2^{O(\ell)}

Proof.

Let us introduce a new register
\mathsf{Q}
supported on states
\ket{z}
for
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}
. Suppose we could implement the following maps:

\displaystyle O:

\displaystyle\ket{z}_{\mathsf{Q}}\ket{x,y,a,w}\mapsto\ket{z}_{\mathsf{Q}}\ket{x,y + z \cdot g(x,a,w),a,w}

(E.7)

\displaystyle W:

\displaystyle\ket{z}_{\mathsf{Q}}\ket{x,y,a,w}\mapsto\ket{z - \chi_{a = 0^{\ell}}}_{\mathsf{Q}}\ket{x,y,a,w},

(E.8)

where
\chi_{a=0^{\ell}}
is the element
1\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}
if
a=0^{\ell}
and is
0\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}
otherwise.
Since

\displaystyle WO:

\displaystyle\ket{z}\ket{x,y,a,w}\mapsto\ket{z-\chi_{a = 0^{\ell}}}\ket{x,y + z \cdot g(x,a,w),a,w},

(E.9)

\displaystyle W^{\dagger}O^{\dagger}:

\displaystyle\ket{z}\ket{x,y,a,w}\mapsto\ket{z+\chi_{a = 0^{\ell}}}\ket{x,y - z \cdot g(x,a,w),a,w},

(E.10)

we can compose these operations to obtain the desired ancilla-controlled implementation:

\displaystyle W^{\dagger}O^{\dagger}WO:\ket{z}\ket{x,y,a,w}

\displaystyle\mapsto\ket{z}\ket{x,y + z \cdot g(x,a,w) - (z - \chi_{a=0^\ell}) \cdot g(x,a,w),a,w}

(E.11)

\displaystyle=\ket{z}\ket{x,y + \chi_{a = 0^{\ell}} f(x),a,w}.

(E.12)

In the last equality, we used the fact that
g
satisfies
\chi_{a=0^{\ell}}\cdot f(x)=\chi_{a=0^{\ell}}\cdot g(x,a,w)
. It remains to show how to implement
O
and
W
.

Implementing O.
For
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}
let
C^{(i)}
be the circuit that applies
C
controlled on element
z
in the
\mathsf{Q}
register satisfying
z\leq i
. Given an implementation of
C
, we can implement
C^{(i)}
by replacing each gate
g
with the gate
g^{(i)}
, which implements
g
controlled on the element
z
in the
\mathsf{Q}
register satisfying
z\leq i
. Since
q=O(1)
, this only requires a constant number of additional elementary gates. Then
O
can be implemented as
O=C^{(q)}C^{(q-1)}\cdots C^{(1)}
.

Implementing
W
.
W
is an
\ell+O(1)
qubit unitary, so it has a
2^{O(\ell)}
-size ancilla-free implementation. ∎

E.1.2
Converting ancilla-controlled to ancilla-independent

Finally, we give a simple transformation from ancilla-controlled implementations to ancilla-independent implementations of
f
. The transformation preserves the ancilla registers but has a size and depth blowup of
2^{\ell}
.

Lemma 33
.

Let
C
